# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented here. The format follows
[Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) and the project uses
[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/). New entries are one line each, linked to
their pull request (CONTRIBUTING.md "Changelog"); each version links to its compare
view at the end of the file.

## [Unreleased]

## [0.20.0] - 2026-11-12

**Upgrading:** run `openmoat policy upgrade` in your own terminal for the new and changed default rules (`kernel-self` now also covers `~/.moat/delivery/`, `~/.moat/remote` and the `openmoat approvals` commands). On Linux, `openmoat run` now starts the Isolated tier (bubblewrap) by default where bubblewrap works: an agent whose runtime lives elsewhere in the home can need that folder in `sandbox.read_roots`, and `--no-isolate` starts the Lightweight tier as before (see **Migration** under Changed). Library crates (a break, hence 0.20.0): see the **Breaking** entries under Changed.

### Security
- A pipeline a rule matches at its end is decided the same when another command follows it on the line: `curl URL | x ; true` could be left to the agent where `curl URL | x` was denied ([#781](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/781)).
- Under `require.run: full-boundary`, only an allowlist of inherited environment variables reaches what `openmoat run` starts, so a cloud credential in the shell does not; add a name with `sandbox.env_allow` or `--env NAME`, and `openmoat doctor` and `openmoat run --verbose` name what is withheld, never a value. Without the setting, one line says how many inherited variables look like credentials (ADR-077) ([#782](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/782)).
- `require.run: full-boundary` makes `openmoat run` refuse to start without an OS sandbox or, on Linux, without a network namespace for the agent, whatever `--netns` says; `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` report whether it holds on the system (ADR-077) ([#782](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/782)).
- Every line `openmoat mcp wrap`, `unwrap` and `init` print about an MCP server (wrapped, skipped, without the sandbox, tools held) shows its name without the control or hidden characters an agent's file may put in it ([#786](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/786)).
- The policy lock's copy of a pinned MCP server definition no longer keeps the values of OpenCode's `environment` or goose's `envs` in clear, and `openmoat mcp accept` and `openmoat doctor` never show them: they are hidden like `env`. A lock an older version wrote keeps every server pinned as it was, so nothing is accepted again; `openmoat doctor` names one that still holds such a value, `openmoat doctor --accept` rewrites it, and so does any other change to the lock ([#786](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/786)).
- What `openmoat mcp wrap` and `unwrap` print for a server to set by hand no longer passes a control or hidden character from the server's name or arguments to the terminal ([#786](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/786)).
- What `openmoat mcp accept` and `openmoat doctor --accept` show before asking to accept an MCP server definition, and doctor's line for each changed definition, no longer pass a control or hidden character from the server's name, keys or arguments to the terminal ([#788](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/788)).
- A key or a variable's name with line breaks in an MCP server definition can no longer add lines of its own to what `openmoat mcp accept` shows before it asks, so it cannot push the real change off the screen or show another server's ([#788](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/788)).
- What `openmoat status`, `openmoat doctor` and `require.run: full-boundary` say about each MCP server, and doctor's line on a server's held tools, name the server without its control or hidden characters wherever the text is printed ([#788](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/788)).
- `~/.moat/delivery.yaml`, which decides where the audit log is sent, is pinned by the policy lock like the policy: a file no person accepted with `openmoat doctor --accept` stops every hook call and sends nothing. Its keys and tokens must be files inside `~/.moat/delivery/` or keychain items (`env:` is refused), the background run gets a cleared environment (no `SSL_CERT_FILE`, no proxy variables), plain `http` goes to loopback only, and link-local, cloud metadata and non-unicast addresses are refused on every resolved address (ADR-081) ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- A sink's header secret is masked in what is delivered in every form an event could hold it, not only as the full header value: the bare token after a `Bearer ` prefix, the token with its spaces trimmed, and for `Basic` credentials the decoded `user:password` and the password alone, so `echo $TOKEN` in an agent's call is not sent to the collector in the clear ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- `kernel-self` denies agents and sandboxed commands reading `~/.moat/delivery/` (and `delivery/` in any `.moat/`), where audit delivery's webhook key and collector token live, so `cat ~/.moat/delivery/webhook.key` is denied; a key or token file elsewhere in the state directory, which the agent may read, is refused ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- The default policy's `kernel-self` rule denies agents reading `~/.moat/remote` (each approval channel's credential under `~/.moat/remote/<channel>/`, and the key that signs requests, answers and the approver list) and running any `openmoat approvals` command, by either name, by path and under the pseudo-terminal wrappers (ADR-078) ([#790](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/790)).
- Remote approval: a call longer than the 1500 characters an approver is shown can only be denied from another device, on every channel; the request records that it was cut, under its signature, and the guard uses no approval for such a request (ADR-078) ([#790](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/790)).
- Remote approval: a call whose shown text redaction changed (a value that looks like a secret, a URL's user and password) can only be denied from another device, since the hidden part may be what runs; the approver is told to approve it at the terminal (ADR-078) ([#790](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/790)).
- Remote approval: an approval is bound to every argument the agent's tool call carries, hashed as canonical JSON, not only to the fields OpenMoat reads, so an approved notebook edit, a host's own write or replace tool, or an unknown tool's call cannot be swapped for one with other content (ADR-078) ([#790](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/790)).
- Remote approval: when a call's approval was used but the call was then refused because its audit row could not be written, the call stays denied and the approver is told "approved, but the call was refused" with the reason, and it is recorded, instead of the approval vanishing silently (ADR-078) ([#790](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/790)).
- Remote approval: a channel's chunked HTTPS response with a chunk size past the 4 MiB response limit is refused, where a size near the largest number could overflow and stop `openmoat approvals listen` (ADR-078) ([#790](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/790)).
- Remote approval: `openmoat approvals listen` answers every reply of a poll even when one fails (it is logged), and only then moves past them on the channel's server, where one failing reply used to drop the rest of that poll (ADR-078) ([#790](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/790)).
- Remote approval: a line break in the call is shown as a line break marked with `⏎`, not as `?`, so `ls foo` followed by `rm -rf ~/work` reads as the two commands it is (ADR-078) ([#790](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/790)).
- `openmoat run` on Linux no longer lets a command reach a pathname datagram socket outside the sandbox (`/dev/log`, journald's socket) through a datagram socket pair, which it could connect again or send to with an address: the seccomp filter now allows `socketpair()` only for stream and sequenced-packet sockets, which Node, Python and Chromium use, and a datagram pair fails with `EPERM` ([#794](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/794)).
- Where the kernel has Landlock ABI 9 (Linux 7.1), `openmoat run` also has Landlock refuse connecting or sending to any pathname Unix socket made outside the sandbox (`EACCES`), beside the seccomp filter; an older kernel keeps the filter alone, as before (ADR-082) ([#794](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/794)).

### Added
- `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` name the exact `openmoat run` command for each agent OpenMoat writes no OS sandbox for, or say why there is none on this system or for that agent (ADR-076) ([#779](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/779)).
- `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` show each MCP server OpenMoat can read as protected or unprotected, with why; under `require.run: full-boundary` an unprotected local server fails the requirement, which is reported because the agent starts its servers itself (ADR-077) ([#782](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/782)).
- `openmoat mcp wrap` reads each agent's own form of a server: OpenCode's single `command` list is wrapped and undone in place, and goose's `cmd` and `args` are read, so `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` report goose's extensions. A YAML file or an OpenCode file with comments is named with what to set by hand and no longer stops the other servers; a goose extension of inline Python is named as having no command to wrap ([#785](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/785)).
- Live audit delivery to a SIEM or log collector: `~/.moat/delivery.yaml` names up to four sinks, OTLP/HTTP logs (JSON) or an HTTPS webhook whose bodies are signed with HMAC-SHA-256 (`X-OpenMoat-Signature`). The hook never waits for it: after answering, the guard starts a detached delivery run, which sends the events after each sink's place in the audit log, every text masked again. A sink that fails is tried again with growing waits; one that answers with an error trying again cannot fix is paused; one that falls more than `max_backlog` events behind is told which events it missed, and the skip is recorded in the audit log (`audit-delivery-overflow`). New commands `openmoat audit deliver status`, `test` and `flush` (`docs/DELIVERY.md`, ADR-081) ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- One oversized event no longer stops audit delivery for good: each event is trimmed after masking to the batch size and at most 256 KiB (long texts cut with a `…[openmoat trimmed N bytes]` marker), a `413` answer halves the events per request, and one event that still gets `413` alone is skipped, recorded (`audit-delivery-too-large`) and sent as a gap, instead of pausing the sink ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- A delivery run that fails as a whole (the audit log or the policy cannot be read) no longer makes every hook call start another: the guard waits a minute, doubling to 15 minutes while it keeps failing, and after any run it starts the next one a second later at the soonest ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- An OTLP collector that answers `200` but rejects records (`partial_success`) no longer counts as having taken them: the records are not sent again, as the OTLP specification asks, but the loss is recorded in the audit log (`audit-delivery-partial`, with the count and the collector's message, masked) and sent to the collector as a gap with the next request ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- A sink that has accepted no event yet (a first try that failed, was refused or found nothing to send) no longer stops audit delivery: its state file holds the place before the first event as `0`, which the next run now reads back instead of refusing as an event id ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- `openmoat doctor` names each agent that runs a tool call when OpenMoat's hook cannot start, crashes or times out ("fails open"), with the way out: start it with `openmoat run` and add `require.run: full-boundary` to the policy. `openmoat status --format json` carries each agent's `hook_failure` facts and their sources, and `docs/LIMITS.md` has a per-agent "If the hook fails" table generated from them (ADR-079) ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- The weekly latest-agent job makes OpenMoat's hook fail (exit 1, binary gone) under each real agent it starts, and fails when an agent runs a call its facts say it blocks (ADR-079) ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- Cline CLI counts as running the call when OpenMoat's hook fails: OpenMoat's script blocks it when `openmoat` is missing or crashes, but Cline runs the call when the script itself cannot start (no `sh`, as on native Windows), so `openmoat doctor` names Cline as failing open rather than promising a block ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- `openmoat run` takes a snapshot of the project's files before the agent starts and after it exits, into OpenMoat's own store outside the project (`--no-snapshot` opts out, recorded); `openmoat restore` puts them back after showing each change and asking, and takes an undo snapshot first; `openmoat snapshot` takes, lists (`--list`) and prunes (`--prune`) them. Secrets and other paths a deny rule names, ignored files and files over 5 MiB are left out and named; both commands are person-only, and the default policy denies them, and reading or changing the store, to agents (ADR-085) ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- A snapshot holds each file's bytes as they are and a restore writes back those bytes, whatever the project's `.gitattributes` say: line-ending conversion (`text`, `eol`), `ident` and `working-tree-encoding` are off in OpenMoat's store, so a `.gitattributes` an agent plants can neither change what comes back nor make a snapshot fail ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- Every `openmoat run` records its own start snapshot, even when the files are as the previous snapshot holds them, so a plain `openmoat restore` goes back to the start of the last run and never undoes an earlier run's work as well ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- Two `openmoat run` in one project, or a run beside a person's `openmoat snapshot` or `openmoat restore`, no longer race on the snapshot store: taking, pruning and restoring hold the store's lock, each snapshot is staged in its own index file, and pruning drops only unreferenced files older than an hour ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- `openmoat restore` cannot be made to write or delete outside the project by a process of the agent that swaps a checked folder for a link while the restore runs: on macOS and Linux every write, rename and delete is made relative to the folder it checked, held open (`openat` with `O_NOFOLLOW`); on Windows each folder is checked again right before each change and any change refuses the path (ADR-085) ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- `openmoat restore` no longer deletes a file that was there but ignored when the snapshot was taken (an agent that stops ignoring `notes.local` cannot get it deleted): each snapshot lists the ignored paths that existed, and the restore leaves them as they are and says so ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- A call held for review can be approved from your phone: `openmoat approvals connect telegram` connects a Telegram bot you made, `openmoat approvals allow telegram:<user-id>` names who may approve, and while `openmoat approvals listen` runs each review is sent there with Approve and Deny buttons. An approval lets that exact call (agent, session, folder, command or file and its content, and the rules that held it) run once, within 5 minutes by default; a deny is never sent, and every failure leaves the review as it was. `openmoat approvals status`, `test`, `remove` and `disconnect` complete it; nothing changes without a channel. Channels are built to be added one folder at a time: each declares what it can do, and OpenMoat shapes the message to it (buttons, or reply codes to send back, and Deny only when a channel cannot show the whole call); approver ids carry the channel's name, so the same user number on two services never counts twice. docs/APPROVALS.md says how to add one (ADR-078) ([#790](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/790)).
- Short-lived brokered secrets (preview): a secret's `source: { command: { run: [...], lifetime: 15m } }` names a program, such as a cloud CLI's token command, that `openmoat proxy` runs for the value, uses it for at most `lifetime` (1 minute to 12 hours, or less when the program prints an expiry) and runs again before it ends, on a thread of its own. Past its deadline a request that needs the value is refused before anything is sent and recorded as `proxy-secret-expired`; a stale value is never sent. The program runs only when a deny `fs.write` rule covers it or only an administrator can change it, never from `/tmp`, pinned by its SHA-256 for the life of the proxy, with no input, a bare environment, a 30-second limit and 64 KiB of output; its output is never quoted and every value it gave is masked in the audit log. `openmoat guard`, audit delivery and Claude Code's broker never run it, and a repository policy or pack cannot add one (`docs/POLICY.md` §2.2, ADR-087) ([#793](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/793)).
- A `command` source's program starts in `/`, never in the agent's project, with an empty home of its own under OpenMoat's state folder; `home: user` gives it your home for a tool that keeps its login there ([#793](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/793)).
- A `command` source's program finds tools in the system's folders only, never its own; every folder above it, up to your home, must be kept from the agent as the file is, and a script's interpreter must pass the same check and is pinned too (`#!/usr/bin/env` is refused). The example is now Vault's CLI, not `gcloud`, whose Python loads from folders you can write ([#793](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/793)).
- The proxy runs a private copy of the bytes it hashed, checked again before each run, never the file at the program's path; a deny rule counts as proof only outside the project and `openmoat run`'s write roots, where only root ownership does ([#793](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/793)).
- On macOS and Linux the time limit ends a `command` source's whole process group, and its output is read with a deadline, so a child it left holding its output neither outlives the run nor keeps a thread of the proxy waiting ([#793](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/793)).
- A `command` source is renewed from the lifetime its value actually got (the shorter of `lifetime` and the expiry it printed), a fifth of it before the end, at least a minute ahead and at most once a minute after an answer, each secret on its own thread; a program that printed an expiry shorter than `refresh_before` was run on every look ([#793](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/793)).
- Under `openmoat run`, a repository policy's deny rules no longer count as proof that the agent cannot change a `command` source's program; only your own policy's do ([#793](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/793)).
- `openmoat guard`'s audit rows do not mask a `command` source's value: OpenMoat does not write the values to a file for it, which would be a copy on disk of the tokens the source keeps short-lived; LIMITS.md says so ([#793](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/793)).
- `openmoat doctor` on Linux names the kernel's Landlock ABI and what `openmoat run` applies from it (each ABI from 4 to 12 with `--verbose`: used, available but not used and why, or not available and what holds instead); `openmoat sandbox show` and `openmoat run --verbose` show the same, `sandbox show --format json` has it under `lightweight.landlock.abi`, and every Linux run writes it to the audit log (rule `run-landlock-abi`) before the agent starts (ADR-082) ([#794](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/794)).
- `docs/PROTECTION.md`: one row per agent on Linux, macOS and Windows of what protects its calls (the hook as protected, preview or none; what the agent does when the hook fails; its own sandbox; the tier `openmoat run` starts it in; its MCP servers; what a command can read inside a run), with the tests and weekly real-agent runs behind each claim. It is generated from the agents' facts and from end-to-end tests marked `// protection:`, a claim nothing backs reads `no evidence yet` and its summary `preview`, and the docs test fails when the page or a mark is wrong; `openmoat doctor --verbose` ends with a pointer to it (ADR-079) ([#795](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/795)).

### Changed
- `docs/COVERAGE.md` now shows all ten risks of the OWASP Top 10 for Agentic Applications (2026), each marked covered, partial or out of scope with a one-line reason that says what is left out. Risks no fixture class maps to show no counts, and a test keeps a row for every risk from ASI01 to ASI10 ([#777](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/777)).
- The README shows OpenMoat's own MoatBench result for 0.19.0 from the benchmark's own repository; the older comparison with other tools is removed ([#778](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/778)).
- At a terminal, `openmoat init` now offers to run the agents' stdio MCP servers through `openmoat mcp proxy`, shows the change and asks before writing; `--no-mcp-proxy` opts out, a no is remembered per definition, and without a person nothing is rewritten (ADR-076) ([#779](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/779)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-hosts`: `HookLayout::Plugin` holds a `&'static dyn PluginConfig`, the new trait that edits a host's plugin configuration, instead of the plugin's `&'static OwnedFile` (`PluginConfig::plugin` returns that file); the `OPENCLAW_CONFIG_FILE`, `OPENCLAW_PLUGIN_ID`, `OPENCLAW_STATE_DIR_NAME` and `OPENCLAW_STATE_DIR_VARIABLE` exports are gone, since what they named is in the host's spec ([#780](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/780)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-core`: `SandboxSettings` has a new public field `env_allow`, and `openmoat-hosts`: `HostSpec` a new public field `run_env`, which breaks code that builds them field by field ([#782](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/782)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-core`: `Require` has a new public field `run` (`Option<RunBoundary>`), which breaks code that builds it field by field ([#782](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/782)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-hosts`: the `CONTINUE_ENV` export is gone; `HostSpec::speaks_variable` holds the variable and `Host::caller_variable` returns it ([#785](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/785)).
- Generic code names no agent for the lock's settings pins, Claude Code's secret masking rows in `sandbox show` and `doctor`, or the variable that says the Continue CLI runs the hook: each comes from the sandbox backend or the host's spec. The lock's file format, what it pins and the output are unchanged ([#785](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/785)).
- The policy lock digests a settings file it pins by OpenMoat's part with the backend of the host whose file it is, found by the file's name, rather than with the first backend that pins by part; what the lock pins and its digests are unchanged ([#786](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/786)).
- Antigravity CLI is checked against a real `agy`: the weekly `latest agents` job records what agy loads and runs ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)), and in its run of 2026-11-12 ([43254846914](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/actions/runs/43254846914)) a real `agy` on Linux and macOS ran OpenMoat's `PreToolUse` hook, which denied a read of a planted key, and blocked the call when the hook binary was missing and when it exited 1 without output. `docs/PROTECTION.md` shows its hook as protected on Linux and macOS, and `openmoat doctor` names it as failing open only when the hook times out, which agy does not document and the job does not try; the weekly job now fails if a later `agy` runs a call when the hook fails.
- The OWASP agentic table in `docs/COVERAGE.md` now states its evidence: end-to-end tests marked for a risk count beside the fixtures, a new section lists each risk's test names and fixture ids, and a risk nothing tests reads `not tested` whatever is claimed for it; the conformance suite fails when a risk claimed as covered or partly covered has no evidence, and when the document differs from what it generates (ADR-079) ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- **Migration:** `openmoat run` on Linux starts the Isolated tier (bubblewrap) by default where bubblewrap works, so commands the agent starts can no longer read `.env` or write `.git/hooks` and `.moat` in the project. The agent sees only the project, the read roots, its executable, the `--write` paths and an empty temp directory; an agent whose runtime lives elsewhere in the home can need that directory in `sandbox.read_roots`. Where bubblewrap is missing or blocked it says why on every start, records `run-isolation-unavailable` in the audit log and starts the Lightweight tier as before. `--no-isolate` starts the Lightweight tier on purpose and records `run-no-isolate`; `openmoat doctor` says which tier applies in its OpenMoat section, without `--verbose`. Under `require.run: full-boundary` the Isolated tier is required on Linux and `--no-isolate` is refused (ADR-080) ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- An MCP server started with `openmoat mcp proxy --sandbox` runs in the Isolated tier on Linux too, so a path outside the project and the read roots is not there for it at all, rather than there and denied ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- The guard's p95 budget is 20 ms for its own decision on Linux CI runners and 45 ms on macOS ones, where the same binary measures 17 to 37 ms median; a row over it warns. The latency check now times the commit before on the same runner on every run, pushes to `main` included, instead of holding `main` to a fixed limit that failed macOS runs whose guard code had not changed, and both latency scripts report the median, p95 and p99 (ADR-080) ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-hosts`: `HookRequest` has a new field, `input`: the call's whole tool arguments as canonical JSON (keys sorted, no whitespace), `None` for an event without a tool call ([#790](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/790)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-core`: `SecretSource` has a `Command` variant (`SecretCommand`), so an exhaustive `match` on it needs an arm, and `SecretCommand::home` (`SecretCommandHome`); `SecretSource::proxy_only` and `describe`, and `lint::write_denied`, are new. `openmoat-proxy` adds `Broker::rotation`, `Rotation` and `RULE_SECRET_EXPIRED`; `openmoat-audit` adds `Store::set_secrets` ([#793](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/793)).

### Fixed
- When `openmoat mcp proxy` refuses every call of a server that was not started, a request id holding U+2028 or a similar line separator is escaped in the reply, so no reader that splits lines on it sees two messages ([#785](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/785)).
- `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` no longer advise `openmoat mcp wrap` for a goose extension of inline Python, which cannot be wrapped, and `openmoat mcp wrap` no longer warns about an OpenCode entry that names no program, such as `{"enabled": false}` or a `url` with no `type` ([#786](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/786)).
- `openmoat mcp wrap <server>` and `unwrap <server>` exit 64 when a server asked for by name is left as it is (one OpenMoat cannot put behind the proxy, or one with a change nobody accepted) instead of printing "no stdio MCP server to wrap" and succeeding, and a file that cannot be read or parsed, or no longer holds the server, fails the command again rather than being named as one to set by hand ([#786](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/786)).
- A wrapped MCP server's variables reach it under `require.run: full-boundary` for OpenCode (`environment`) and goose (`envs`) as they do under `env`, and a definition is read in the form its agent reads it: a stray `cmd` beside `command` and `args` no longer leaves a wrapped server without its `--host` or stops `openmoat mcp unwrap` and `openmoat uninstall` from restoring it ([#786](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/786)).
- A long agent session that moves to another folder no longer has every call denied with `kernel-error` while OpenMoat reads the session's earlier calls again: the session's taint is kept for every directory, and only calls recorded without a working directory keep a directory's own ([#787](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/787)).
- Working out a session's taint classifies each earlier call once instead of twice, so a session read again after an upgrade or a policy edit catches up in fewer calls ([#787](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/787)).
- `openmoat doctor` lists the calls the guard denied in the last day because it could not decide them (`kernel-error`), with the latest one's time, agent and reason (not its session id) and what to run next, so it no longer says "All good" while the guard refuses every call ([#787](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/787)).
- `openmoat doctor --accept` over a drifted lock no longer exits 64 on the line about MCP server values an older version kept in clear: the re-pin has already written the lock without them, and the line is now decided from the lock as it is on disk ([#788](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/788)).
- `openmoat mcp wrap <server>` and `unwrap <server>` exit 64 with a line naming the server when the name matches no server in the agents' own files (a typo), and `wrap` does so for a named remote server, which has nothing to wrap, instead of printing "no stdio MCP server to wrap" and succeeding; a named server already wrapped still succeeds ([#788](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/788)).
- Under `openmoat run`, Claude Code's Bash tool no longer fails on every call when OpenMoat is installed under the home directory (`~/.cargo/bin`, Homebrew on Linux): Claude Code's sandbox, with the `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories` that `openmoat init` sets, hides that directory, and the tool-shell wrapper was a link to it. The wrapper's directory in the system temp directory now holds a copy of the binary, so it starts with nothing outside that directory (ADR-075) ([#789](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/789)).

## [0.19.0] - 2026-11-09

**Upgrading:** run `openmoat policy upgrade` in your own terminal for the new and changed default rules, then `openmoat init` once: hooks now carry OpenMoat's state directory in their command, and hooks of the old form show as out of date until then. Inside `openmoat run`, commands no longer read the shared temp directories: a tool that reads a fixed path in `/tmp` written by another program now fails there (LIMITS.md). `openmoat doctor` shows the mode and owner of the state files and reports a binary from a different release. Library crates (a break, hence 0.19.0): see the **Breaking** entries under Changed.

### Security
- `openmoat doctor` checks the mode and owner of the state directory and each file in it that decides or records a call (lock, policy, approvals, trust and host records, audit log, keys): a file other users may write, or one another user owns, is a problem with the `chmod` or `chown` that fixes it, one they may read or a symbolic link is a warning, and `--verbose` lists every file's mode and owner. Not checked on Windows (ADR-071) ([#772](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/772)).
- The lock records the SHA-256 and release of the binary the hooks run, and `openmoat guard` compares the file it runs from with it (hashed once per change of the file, cached beside the lock): other bytes under the same release deny every call until `openmoat init`, and `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` show it; an upgrade to another release keeps working and is only reported until `openmoat init` records it (ADR-071) ([#772](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/772)).
- `openmoat init` writes the state directory into every hook it installs (`guard --host <id> --home <dir>`, pinned by the lock with the hook file), and `openmoat guard` decides with that directory whatever the agent's environment says: a `MOAT_HOME` in the hook's environment that names another directory makes it deny the call. Before, an agent that built its hooks' environment from a file or setting OpenMoat does not guard could point the guard at another policy and lock. Hooks written by earlier releases still work and show as out of date in `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` until you run `openmoat init` (ADR-071) ([#772](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/772)).
- A shell command that creates or replaces a project file an agent reads as its hook settings (`.codex/config.toml`, `opencode.json`, `.pi/settings.json`, by a redirect, `tee`, `cp`, `mv` or `sed -i`) is denied (`project-settings`) where that agent would load it and OpenMoat is set up for it, because what it leaves cannot be known before it runs. Reading those files, removing them, moving them away, and same-named files elsewhere keep their verdict; changes through the agent's file tools are judged by their content as before ([#773](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/773)).
- Under `openmoat run`, tool commands of Claude Code, OpenCode, goose, Hermes Agent, OpenClaw, the Continue CLI and pi no longer receive the run's relay token, and a command cannot read it from the agent that started it (macOS: the sandbox denies `kern.procargs*`; Linux: no grant covers `/proc`, and a wrapped command gets a Landlock layer of its own). Codex, Cursor, GitHub Copilot CLI, Gemini CLI, the Cline CLI and the Antigravity CLI start tool commands in a way OpenMoat cannot yet tell from their hooks, so theirs still do. `openmoat doctor` names both groups and `openmoat bench --report` tries the attack (ADR-075) ([#774](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/774)).
- `echo … | base64 <-d | sh ; true` was held for review where the same line without `; true` was denied: a decoder's dash-word after a redirection now counts as its flag ([#775](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/775)).
- `openmoat run` no longer lets commands read the shared temp directories: `/tmp` and macOS's temp folder are left out of its read roots (the run keeps its own temp directory; macOS keeps its per-user caches), which also closes Kerberos ticket caches in `/tmp` on Linux without a network namespace (ADR-018) ([#775](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/775)).

### Added
- Antigravity CLI (`agy`) support: `openmoat init` writes a named `openmoat` entry with one `PreToolUse` hook to `~/.gemini/config/hooks.json`, shared with the Antigravity app and IDE. Every documented tool is mapped by what it does; a review is sent as `force_ask` so a saved "Always Allow" cannot answer it, and a saved `toolPermission: always-proceed` turns one into a deny. The default policy keeps the agent from changing its hook, settings, MCP and plugin files and from reading its MCP OAuth tokens. Built from the official documentation (no version stated) and not yet checked against a running `agy`: what the host does when the hook fails is not documented, so treat a failing hook as letting the call run ([#774](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/774)).

### Changed
- **Breaking** `openmoat-hosts`: `HookFile::entry`, `HookFile::upsert`, `HookFile::state`, `yaml_hooks::upsert`, `ConsentFile::upsert` and `ConsentFile::holds` take a `&GuardCommand` (the binary and the state directory the hook names) instead of the binary as `&str`, and `OwnedFile::text` takes the state directory as a second argument; the crate exports `GuardCommand`, `HOME_FLAG`, `encode_home` and `decode_home` ([#772](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/772)).
- The row that ties an agent session to an `openmoat run` now says that the session's rows come through the run's relay, which any program in the run's sandbox can use, so they show what was asked from inside the run, not that the agent asked it. THREAT_MODEL, LIMITS and ADR-071 say why the relay token cannot be bound to the agent's hook process on any OS today, and what the run still refuses (ADR-071) ([#772](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/772)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-hosts`: `Host` gains `AntigravityCli`, so an exhaustive `match` on `Host` needs the new arm; `HookFile` gains `section`, `section_switch` and `turned_off`, and `Unseen` gains `file` ([#774](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/774)).
- The end-to-end tests run an MCP server that tries to read a planted secret by a relative path, an absolute path and a symbolic link out of its folder and to write outside the project: `openmoat mcp proxy --sandbox` is shown to stop each by the files on disk, and the same calls reach the files without it, as `docs/LIMITS.md` now says ([#775](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/775)).

### Fixed
- `openmoat run` on Linux no longer refuses to start when `TMPDIR` is so long that its network namespace's socket could not be bound; the proof report's OS rows now run on Linux with bubblewrap (ADR-065) ([#775](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/775)).

## [0.18.0] - 2026-11-09

**Upgrading:** run `openmoat policy upgrade` in your own terminal for the new and changed default rules (`kerberos-tickets`, `kernel-self` for `openmoat proxy ca` and `openmoat serve`, and the release-age and folder-contents changes); it also brings `openmoat allow --dir` grants from an earlier release up to date, so they follow the current protections (it asks first). Then run `openmoat init` once: it rewrites Cursor's hook command with the binary path single-quoted and pins programs from absolute `PATH` entries only. Run `openmoat mcp wrap` again for each wrapped MCP server so it carries `--host` (a server wrapped before keeps accepting any agent until then), and `openmoat mcp accept` once: the gateway now pins a server's `initialize` instructions, server information and prompt and resource listings, and holds a changed one. `proxy.key` and the proxy CA key must be private (`chmod 600`); `openmoat doctor` names one that is not. New optional settings: `require:` (a least protection level), `taint.metadata_tools` and `taint.defined_servers`, and `openmoat allow --for <duration>`; an `approved.yaml` holding an end is written as `version: 2`, which an earlier release refuses. Library crates (a break, hence 0.18.0): see the **Breaking** entries under Changed.

### Security
- A directory allowed with `openmoat allow --dir` no longer keeps only the protections of the day it was allowed: the grant stores the directory, and every write the current policy holds for review stays held inside it (a `.git`, CI files, the editors' configuration, `.npmrc`, `.cargo/config.toml`, a virtual environment's `bin/`, `.devcontainer` and whatever a later policy adds); deny rules still win and an exact-path approval still answers its review. A directory allowed with an earlier release is listed by `openmoat doctor` and `openmoat status` until `openmoat policy upgrade` brings it up to date, which only adds protection; `openmoat allowed --format json` lists directory grants under `directories` ([#765](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/765)).
- OpenMoat no longer runs, pins or records a program found through a relative `PATH` entry (`.`, `node_modules/.bin`): the `--version` probes of `init`, `status` and `doctor` (which OpenMoat Bar runs every minute), the pinned programs `init` records, the binary path written into hooks, the agent `openmoat run` starts by name, and the `git` that `provenance` and `policy diff` run all come from the absolute entries only, so a repository with a planted `claude` or `git` cannot get it run from there (ADR-067) ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- On Linux, the Codex profile also denies every letter case of its short workspace deny globs (`**/.env`, `**/.env.*`, `**/.envrc`, `**/.moat/**`), so a file spelled `.ENV` in a directory that folds case is hidden too; longer globs are reported as `codex.linux-case-variants` ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- Cursor's hook command writes the OpenMoat binary path in single quotes on macOS and Linux, so a `$`, backtick or `\` in the path is never run by Cursor's shell; `openmoat init` rewrites an existing entry, and `doctor` reads both forms ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- `openmoat proxy ca init` is person-only in the default policy too (`kernel-self`), by either name, by path and under the pseudo-terminal wrappers; `expect`, Python's `pty`, `tmux`, `screen` and `osascript` running `proxy ca`, `proxy install` or `proxy uninstall` in a quoted command line are denied as well. The command already refused an agent among its parents; the hook now denies it before it starts. `openmoat policy upgrade` adds the lines to an existing policy ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- `openmoat proxy` no longer counts a name pattern with a port whose host part is all hex letters (`fac*:5432`) as an address pattern: only a host part that starts with a digit, or IPv6 text without a port, opens a private or unique local address, so such an allow cannot name `fd00::1:5432`. The policy engine already kept a pattern with a port from matching IPv6 text; a test now pins both layers ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- `openmoat proxy` refuses the reserved range `240.0.0.0/4` and the deprecated site-local range `fec0::/10` unless an allow rule names the address, as it refuses `10.0.0.0/8`; before, both were reachable through a wildcard allow, and `fec0::/10` was treated as public space. It also refuses a TLS tunnel whose `ClientHello` carries an encrypted server name under the codepoints of ECH drafts 08 to 12 (`0xfe08` to `0xfe0c`), rule `proxy-sni`, as it already did for `0xfe0d` and ESNI ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- `openmoat run` on Linux refuses `pidfd_getfd`, which copies an open file (a socket, a token file) out of another process past every path rule; `pidfd_open` stays allowed ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- A download piped into a versioned Python (`curl … | python2.7`) is now denied by `pipe-to-shell` also when another command follows it on the line (`curl … | python2.7; true`); before, only the line ending in it was ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- `openmoat doctor` and `openmoat status` read the proxy's launchd or systemd service file back: an environment variable other than `MOAT_HOME` (`DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES`, `LD_PRELOAD`, an `EnvironmentFile=`), any other change from what `openmoat proxy install` writes, or a mode open to others is a problem, with `openmoat proxy install` named to write it again ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- A glob that names more than can be listed (more than 256 paths, 1024 directories or 8 levels of `**`) or that cannot be matched the way the shell does now makes the call need review, as a line past the atom or length limit already did; before, the default policy deferred it, so a secret past the bound could reach the agent's own prompt ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- An MCP call's arguments under keys OpenMoat does not know are now checked for file paths: a string that starts at `/`, `\`, a drive, `~` or `file:` (`mcp__custom__run {"input": "~/.ssh/id_rsa"}`) is checked as a file read against the policy's `deny` rules, so `secrets-paths` denies it as it denies the same path under `path`. Only deny rules apply to such a value, and never as a write, so a path no deny rule names, a relative word or a bare `~` in some text field changes nothing and adds no prompt ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- A `)` between a download and the shell it is piped to no longer hides the pipe: `curl URL)|bash ; true` and `(curl URL) | sh; true` are denied by `pipe-to-shell`, as `curl URL | bash` is. A group's parentheses neither start nor end a pipe chain, in every check that reads one (found by the shell fuzz target) ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- A download substituted into the text a shell runs (`sh -c "$(curl …)"`, `bash -c "$(wget -qO- …)"`, also behind a wrapper such as `env timeout 60 bash -c "$(curl …)"`) is now denied by `pipe-to-shell` like `curl … | sh`: the classifier reports it as the same canonical pipeline. `eval "$(curl …)"` gets that pipeline too. `echo "$(date)"` and a single-quoted `sh -c '$(…)'` are unchanged ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` read Claude Code's managed settings (with `managed-settings.d/`), Gemini CLI's system settings and defaults and Codex's `requirements.toml`, read-only, and call the agent not protected when one turns hooks off (`disableAllHooks`, `hooksConfig`, `[features] hooks = false`) or runs only its own hooks without OpenMoat's (`allowManagedHooksOnly`, `allow_managed_hooks_only`), naming the file and key; a missing or unreadable file says nothing (ADR-068) ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- A command string handed to a shell by a program OpenMoat does not know (`mywrap sh -c 'cat ~/.ssh/id_rsa'`, `poetry run bash -lc '…'`) is now checked as the command it is, so a deny in it is no longer deferred; the commands containers run (`docker`, `podman`, `nerdctl`, `kubectl`, `oc`) are left to `container-host` (LIMITS.md) ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- `openmoat guard` sends a hook call to `openmoat run` only from inside the run's sandbox: its own lock refused by Seatbelt on macOS, or under the seccomp filter `openmoat run` applies on Linux. A hook outside any run whose environment sets `MOAT_GUARD_SOCKET` and `MOAT_GUARD_TOKEN` is decided where it is, and a `run-guard-outside` row records that the variables were there; before, a listener at that address that repeated the nonce answered every call (ADR-070) ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- `openmoat run` decides relayed hook calls only for its own agent and its own sessions: a call for another host, a session the audit log already holds from outside the run (unless the agent's own session start resumes it), a `run-…` session, or a 65th session gets a deny and a `run-guard-host` or `run-guard-session` row. A relayed call no longer leaves an MCP ticket. Before, any code in the run's sandbox (a script, a test runner) could use `MOAT_GUARD_TOKEN` to write rows under any host and session and to leave tickets an `openmoat mcp proxy` outside the run would take (ADR-070) ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- An agent's write that turns a host's hook off in a project's settings is denied (`project-settings`) instead of needing review: `[features] hooks = false` in `.codex/config.toml`, a `.pi/settings.json` `extensions` entry that excludes OpenMoat's extension, an `opencode.json` plugin entry that removes OpenMoat's plugin. The guard works out the content a `Write`, an `Edit`, pi's and OpenCode's `edit` or an `apply_patch` leaves and asks the host's own settings reader; every other change to those files still needs review ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- `openmoat run` on Linux compares each deny rule's path where it resolves, as it does the `--write` paths it grants: with `~/.claude` a link, `--write ~/.claude` now keeps `settings.json` in the linked folder read-only in the network namespace and under `--isolate`, Landlock's list of denies it cannot enforce names it, and without a namespace the run refuses as for an unlinked folder ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- `openmoat mcp accept` now shows tag characters, variation selectors, the soft hyphen, invisible operators and every other format character as `?`, and says how many invisible characters a tool definition holds, so a description that looks harmless in the terminal cannot hide text the model reads; such a tool is never pinned on first sight, even under `mcp: { trust_first_sight: true }` ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- The MCP gateway now reads a wrapped server's error answer to a tool call as it reads a result: a host, path or command in the error's `message` or `data` is injected input for the session (`injected-input`), and a piece of it carried to another server's sending tool needs review (`mcp-cross-server`); an error the gateway cannot record reaches the agent as OpenMoat's error instead ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- The MCP gateway now refuses a wrapped server's sampling request that asks for the session's context (`includeContext` other than `none`), keeps the text of the server's other requests to the agent as untrusted content of the session it serves (`injected-input`, and the session counts as having read untrusted content), and passes the agent's answer to the server only for a request the server sent ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- `openmoat mcp proxy` now pins a server's `initialize` instructions and server information, and each entry of its prompt, resource and resource-template listings, with its tools: new or changed instructions are taken out of the answer the agent gets, a new or changed prompt or resource is left out of its listing (and a held prompt is not filled in), until `openmoat mcp accept` shows what changed; a new version number alone asks nothing, and a server accepted before this release is not asked about again. Accepted instructions are kept as untrusted content of each session that calls the server (`injected-input`, and the session counts as having read untrusted content) (ADR-069) ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- `openmoat mcp wrap` now writes `--host <agent>` into each wrapped definition (and adds it to one wrapped before, the next time you run it), and the MCP gateway takes only that agent's hook decisions, preferring the session it already served, so two agents or two sessions sending the same call each have the result kept for their own session ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- `openmoat run` on Linux keeps commands from creating an agent's hook settings the project lacks (`.claude/settings.local.json`, `.gemini/settings.json`, `.github/copilot/settings.local.json`, `.codex/hooks.json`, `.cursor/hooks.json`, …), with which the next session would run without OpenMoat's hook: in the network namespace and under `--isolate` each is a read-only `{}` for the session, its folders pinned, removed when the agent exits; without a namespace `openmoat run` names each that appeared when the agent exits ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- A newline right after `|` no longer hides the pipe from the check of a program that runs what it reads: `curl URL |` followed by `ruby` on the next line, then another command, is denied by `pipe-to-shell` as `curl URL | ruby` is (found by the shell fuzz target) ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- `openmoat proxy` searches the plain-HTTP answer to a request a `plain_http: true` secret's value went into for that value, as it already searched a terminated tunnel's answer: the connection closes before the value reaches the agent, and a `proxy-secret` row records it. Before, an owner host that echoed the request (a debugging endpoint, an error page quoting a header) handed the agent the value over plain HTTP ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- The proxy session key (`~/.moat/proxy.key`) and the proxy CA's key are refused, not used, when others may read them (any group or other mode bit) or replace them (a folder others may write). `openmoat proxy`, the guard and `openmoat run` stop with the file, its mode and the fix (`chmod 600`, or make a new key), and `openmoat doctor` lists such a key. Before, a key restored from a backup with other modes, or kept on a shared disk, was used as it was, and another user who could read it could sign session credentials or certificates ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- `openmoat run` no longer lets a repository switch the package release age off: a `before=` in the project's `.npmrc` or `exclude-newer` in its `pyproject.toml` or `uv.toml` is kept only when it is a stricter (earlier) date, the TOML key is parsed instead of searched for as text, the person's own `~/.npmrc` and `~/.config/uv/uv.toml` are honoured, and a project with a `uv.lock`, which still gets no `UV_EXCLUDE_NEWER`, is named on every start ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- `openmoat run` gives each run a temp directory of its own (`openmoat-run-<random>`, mode `0700`, the agent's `TMPDIR`, removed when the run ends) instead of granting the shared one, so commands no longer write other programs' and sessions' temp files ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- A new deny rule, `kerberos-tickets`, denies Kerberos ticket caches (`/tmp/krb5cc_*`) to the agent's tools (`hook_only` for the host sandboxes), and `openmoat run` applies it in its sandbox with the cache `KRB5CCNAME` names added; on Linux the network namespace hides the ones in a read root ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- Claude in Chrome's tab lists no longer escape session taint when the tool comes from a server an agent's MCP configuration defines under the name `claude-in-chrome`: the real one is provided by Claude Code itself, so a configured server of that name is another program, and once accepted every result of it taints the session; `openmoat mcp accept` says so when it shows such a server, and `taint: { defined_servers: [...] }` turns the built-in list off by hand (POLICY.md §4.1, ADR-069) ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- Refusals made before a credential is verified no longer write one audit row each: `openmoat proxy`'s and `openmoat run`'s `407` (`proxy-session`), a request head the proxy cannot read (`proxy-request`) and a hook relay call with a wrong token (`run-guard-token`) are recorded at once the first time, then counted, with one row per 10 seconds per kind that says how many it stands for. Before, any local process could grow the audit log without limit until it could no longer be written, after which the proxy and the guard refused everything (ADR-070). `openmoat-proxy` exports the counter as `coalesce` ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).

### Added
- A temporary proof-of-concept setting for ADR-062 step 0, not a feature: with `OPENMOAT_ADR062_POC=1`, `openmoat run` terminates HTTPS to `api.github.com` (with a proxy CA that `openmoat proxy ca init`, run with the same setting, made to cover it) and refuses `POST /gists` and anything posted under it with rule `proxy-request-rule` and an audit row, before anything reaches GitHub; every other request there passes unchanged. `scripts/ci/adr062-poc.py` measures which clients accept the CA and the latency added. It goes away when the `requests:` policy section replaces it ([#764](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/764)).
- `openmoat bench --report` answers six security questions ("can an agent read ~/.ssh/id_rsa?", "can a build script read an agent's credentials?", "can MCP output steer a write?", "can data go out through an allowed host?", "can the agent turn OpenMoat off or edit its policy?", "does a hook crash or timeout fail closed?") one row per layer that holds each: the policy decision, the host hook, the OS sandbox (the attack run for real under `openmoat run` in a throwaway home) and the network boundary (the proxy's decision). Each row is PASS, FAIL or NOT TESTED HERE with the reason, never a pass for a layer that did not run here, with the OpenMoat version, the system and its version, each agent's version when found, and the scenario or probe id that reproduces it. Text, `--format json` and `--format markdown`; the command exits 1 when an exercised layer fails, for CI. `--scratch-dir` puts the throwaway home elsewhere (on Linux the OS rows need one outside `/tmp`) (ADR-065, [#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- MoatBench scenario `file-tool-reads-agent-login`: reading another agent's login file with a file tool is denied (`agent-credentials`) ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- A required protection level: a `require:` section in your policy (`protection: hook-and-os-sandbox` or `hook-only`, and per agent under `agents:`) makes OpenMoat deny every call of an agent below that level (`protection-required`), with a block message that says what is missing and the one step that fixes it (`openmoat sandbox sync`, `openmoat run`); `openmoat run` refuses to start a program it cannot bring to the level, and `openmoat status`, `openmoat doctor` and the home screen show the level each agent must be at and whether it is met. A repository policy or a policy pack can raise the level and never lower it. Off unless you set it (ADR-064) ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- `openmoat serve` shows what `openmoat status`, `show`, `report` and `why` show in a browser on this machine, and the same JSON their `--format json` prints under `/api/`. It listens on 127.0.0.1 or `[::1]` only and refuses any other address, prints a link with a token made for the run once, and answers only requests that carry it, name the server as `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` or `[::1]` and come from no other site; it is read-only (`GET` only, no path changes anything), sets no cookie and no CORS header, and its pages run no script under a strict Content Security Policy. Like `openmoat allow`, it refuses to start when an agent started it, and the default policy denies `openmoat serve` to agents (`kernel-self`) ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- The cross-host consistency suite now also decides calls made after a secret read or untrusted content (session taint) and with the state directory moved, and sends one MCP tool call per agent the way that agent sends it, so an agent whose payload drops a fetch, a path or an MCP tool's arguments fails the suite instead of getting a different verdict ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- `docs/COVERAGE.md` maps each threat class to the OWASP Top 10 for Agentic Applications risks it is a case of (ASI02 to ASI06) and counts the fixtures per risk, and MoatBench's obfuscation set has four more scenarios: `$IFS` in place of a space, a here-string handed to a shell, `xargs sh -c` running a download, and `eval` of a string built from variables ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- The sandbox differential suite also checks Codex's permissions profile, Cursor's `sandbox.json` and the masks `openmoat run` adds inside its grants on Linux: every sampled path the policy denies must be denied there too or named in the backend's report ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- The weekly check of each agent's latest release now proves Claude Code's `SessionStart` hook and its `PostToolUse` hook (a call to a host an earlier call's output named is held as `injected-input`), and copies every payload a real agent sends `openmoat guard` into its artefact, scrubbed, for the adapters' captured fixtures; only that job's build (`--features capture-payloads`) copies anything, no release does ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- `openmoat allow --site`, `--dir` and `--always` take `--for <duration>` (`30m`, `2h`, `3d`, `1w`, at most 366 days): the rule ends on its own, and from then on the call gets the policy's own decision again. Without `--for` a rule lasts until removed, as before. An end that cannot be read, or a clock set back to before the rule was given, counts as ended; granting the same site, directory or command again replaces its end. `openmoat allowed` shows when each rule ends and marks ended ones `EXPIRED` (`--format json`: `expires`), `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` name rules that ended or end within a day, and `openmoat allow --prune` removes the ended ones. `approved.yaml` holding an end is written as `version: 2`, which earlier releases refuse rather than read as permanent ([#769](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/769)).
- `--since` (`report`, `show`, `audit`, `replay`) also reads minutes and seconds (`30m`) ([#769](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/769)).

### Changed
- **Breaking** `openmoat-core`: `Notice` has a new public field `next` (`Option<String>`, the person's step where the deciding check has its own; set it to `None` where you build a notice yourself, or start from `Notice::default()`), and `openmoat-hosts` exports `NEVER_SUGGESTED`, the words a block message never suggests ([#763](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/763)).
- OpenMoat's own checks now send the same block message as a titled policy rule: when a call is held because it uses a command, host or path from a page the agent read (`injected-input`), after the session read untrusted content (`session-taint`), or denied because OpenMoat's own files changed (`kernel-integrity`), and for the other built-in checks, the agent and you read what was stopped, why, and one step for you ("approve it in this prompt only if you asked for it yourself", "run `openmoat doctor` in your terminal to see what changed"); `openmoat why` and `openmoat watch` show their titles too (ADR-059) ([#763](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/763)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-proxy`: `Proxy` has a new public field `requests` (`&Requests`; set it to `&Requests::default()` where you build a proxy yourself), and `Requests` (a host's `deny` request rules, `Requests::deny` and `Requests::hosts`), `RequestRuleError` and `RULE_REQUEST_RULE` (`proxy-request-rule`) are new, for the ADR-062 step 0 proof of concept ([#764](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/764)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-core`: `TaintSettings` has a new public field `metadata_tools` (`Vec<String>`, the MCP tools whose results do not taint a session beyond the built-in list; set it to `Vec::new()` where you build the settings yourself, or start from `TaintSettings::default()`) ([#766](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/766)).
- A browser's tab list no longer taints the session: after only Claude in Chrome's `tabs_context_mcp`, `tabs_create_mcp`, `tabs_close_mcp`, `list_connected_browsers`, `select_browser`, `switch_browser`, `resize_window` or `shortcuts_list`, writes to memory, instruction and CI files are decided as in a clean session, and files the session writes carry no provenance taint. A host, path or command in a tab's title or URL is still injected input (`injected-input`, naming the tab tool). Everything else still taints the session as before: the page-content tools (`get_page_text`, `read_page`, `find`, `navigate`, `javascript_tool`, the console and network logs, screenshots, `form_input`, `file_upload`, `upload_image`, `gif_creator`), every other MCP server's tools, fetches, web searches and unknown tools. `taint: { metadata_tools: [...] }` adds another server's metadata tools by full name (POLICY.md §4.1, ADR-057 amendment) ([#766](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/766)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-core`: `Policy` has new public fields `require` (`Option<Require>`) and `require_added` (the requirements a repository policy or pack merged in), and `RepoPolicy` a new public field `require` (`Option<Require>`); set them to `None` and an empty `Vec` where you build one field by field. New exports `Require`, `RequiredLevel`, `RequiredBy`, `Requirement` and `Policy::required_for`, `Policy::add_requirement` ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- Tests pin the nesting limit (4 levels) and the brace limit (256 words): at the limit a command is classified as written, past it the call needs review; the shell fuzz target also checks that appending `; true` to a decided line never lowers its verdict ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-core`: `Action::McpTool` has a new field `value_reads` (`Vec<String>`, paths only a value under an unknown argument key looks like, checked against `deny` rules only; set it to `Vec::new()` where you build the variant yourself, or start from `Action::mcp`). `openmoat-sdk`'s `McpCall` gains the same field, the `value_reads` builder and the optional `value_reads` key in its JSON form ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- `openmoat bench --report` names its hook rows "host model (golden payloads)" (`host-model` in JSON): they come from OpenMoat's own model of each host's payload and reply, not from running the host. The host's own handling of a failed hook is its own "real host" row, always NOT TESTED HERE, and the report no longer prints the version of a program named like the host, since no host program ran ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-hosts`: `HookRequest` has a new public field `patch` (`Option<String>`, the `apply_patch` envelope a call applies; set it to `None` where you build a request yourself). `SettingsReader` has a new method `project_switched_off` with a default; `openmoat-core` exports `patch_replay` and `PatchReplayed`, which work out the content a patch leaves in each file ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- CI runs `openmoat run` as a stock Ubuntu gets it, Landlock and seccomp without bubblewrap, end to end: the port-only notice, `--netns=require` exiting 64, the refused `--isolate` and `--write` outside the project, and the hostile project scripts of `docs/EVIDENCE.md`. Every other Linux job installs bubblewrap, so that path had not run in CI before ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-core`: `TaintSettings` has a new public field `defined_servers` (`Vec<String>`, the MCP servers a configuration defines; the built-in metadata tools do not apply while it names `BUILTIN_METADATA_SERVER`; set it to `Vec::new()` where you build the settings yourself, or start from `TaintSettings::default()`), and `BUILTIN_METADATA_SERVER` is exported ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).

### Fixed
- `openmoat policy upgrade` no longer lists a narrow pattern the default folded into a broader one (0.17.0's `.vscode/tasks.json` into `.vscode/**`) as "Fewer interruptions": a pattern a rule still covers is counted as "now matched by a broader one", and the `openmoat watch` example in USAGE.md marks a deferred call `→` ([#765](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/765)).
- The check for an agent among a command's parent processes recognises an agent started through an interpreter with options before its script (`node --no-warnings …/@github/copilot/index.js`, `bun --smol …`, `python3 -X utf8 …`), so person-only commands refuse it as they refuse `node …/index.js` ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- `openmoat init` from a shell whose `PATH` finds none of the programs it pins (an empty `PATH`) refuses with exit 64 and writes nothing, instead of recording a search path that pins nothing and reporting success; over an existing snapshot it keeps that one and says so ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- In a long Claude Code session whose history OpenMoat's cache had not caught up on (after an upgrade, a policy change, or in a new directory), calls were denied with `kernel-error` until a few calls had read through the backlog. The `PostToolUse` hook now reads on through the session after each call ran, so the next call is decided within its own time; a call that still runs out of time says plainly that it was stopped, not judged, and that running it again reads on ([#767](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/767)).
- `openmoat sandbox show` for Codex now lists the reads its profile takes away: a write-deny glob has no read-only form, so sandboxed commands cannot read those files either, and a policy that allows by default gets only the paths it lists ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- A lock that pins a Codex `config.toml` both whole and by OpenMoat's profile part is caught as a bug when it is saved (in debug builds and tests), and an audit-log migration that finds another process already migrated decides from the version it read under the lock, refusing a newer schema, instead of starting over ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- THREAT_MODEL.md no longer says Codex hides a command's `workdir`: it describes how `openmoat guard` decides a call from the session's directory when Codex sends a `cwd` outside the project, and the paragraph is now generated from each agent's spec so it cannot go stale again ([#768](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/768)).
- `openmoat bench` counts a run as `sandboxed` only when every step the hook let through is one the scenario expects OpenMoat's OS sandbox to hold; one marked step no longer counts the whole run. The column is marked as expected, not run, under the table ([#770](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/770)).

## [0.17.0] - 2026-11-07

**Upgrading:** run `openmoat policy upgrade` in your own terminal for the new and changed default rules (`managed-settings`, `container-host`, editor and package-manager configuration, virtual environments and dev containers in `runs-later` and `persistence`, and a `title` and `why` on every deny and review rule). Then run `openmoat init` once: it adds Cursor's `beforeTabFileRead` hook and the Cline CLI's `PostToolUse` file, and finds the Claude Code the VS Code extension bundles; `openmoat doctor` reports a hook entry as out of date until then. The audit log schema is unchanged. New optional policy settings: a rule's `title:` and `why:`, and the `staging:` section; an older OpenMoat rejects a policy that uses them. Text output now marks verdicts `✓ allow`, `→ defer`, `? review` and `✕ deny`: a script that matched `⛔`, `❓` or `✔` should match the verdict word or read `--format json`. Library crates (a break, hence 0.17.0): `openmoat-core` `Decision` gains `notice`, `RuleGroup` gains `title` and `why`, `Policy` gains `staging`; `openmoat-hosts` `HostSpec` gains `bundled`; see the Changed entries.

### Security
- `docker`, `podman` and `nerdctl` commands that give a container the host (`--privileged`, a host namespace, `--cap-add`, `--device`, `--security-opt`, the host's root or the Docker socket mounted in) need review (`container-host`); other container runs still defer ([#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- Claude Code in VS Code and JetBrains: `openmoat init` now finds the Claude Code the VS Code extension bundles (and the same extension in VS Code Insiders, VS Code Server, Cursor, Windsurf and VSCodium) from the editor's `extensions.json`, so a person who uses only the extension gets `onFailure: "block"` and a hook that fails blocks the call; an older copy beside it leaves the key out. `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` list each bundled copy, warn about one too old, and warn about Claude Code before 2.1.310 on Windows, where a hook's block could still let a call through without Git for Windows or Git Bash (ADR-060, [#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- The editor tools Claude Code uses in VS Code and JetBrains are decided by what they do: `mcp__ide__getDiagnostics` and `mcp__claude-vscode__getDiagnostics` read the file their `uri` names, so a secret's diagnostics are denied like a `Read` of it (and a project file's are no longer refused as a network call), and `mcp__ide__executeCode`, which runs Python in the notebook's kernel, needs review (ADR-060, [#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- `runs-later` now holds for review an agent's writes to the editors' project configuration, which the editor runs: all of `.vscode/` (tasks, launch configurations, MCP servers, settings that name a program; before, only `tasks.json`), `*.code-workspace`, all of `.idea/` and JetBrains' shared `*.run.xml` run configurations, and the `.vscode` and `.idea` folders themselves. Reading them stays allowed. Existing policies get it with `openmoat policy upgrade` (ADR-060, [#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- Cursor runs Claude Code's hooks too, with its own input: OpenMoat now reads such a call as Cursor's `preToolUse` (a `Grep` naming a secret in `file_path` is denied, not read as a search of the project) and answers a `review` as a deny, since Cursor runs a `preToolUse` call answered `ask` (ADR-060, [#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- Cursor's Tab (inline completion) reads are checked: `openmoat init` installs `beforeTabFileRead`, which refuses Tab reading a secret and lets every other file through; run `openmoat init` to add it (ADR-060, [#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- Injected input (ADR-057) is kept for more than Claude Code: the MCP gateway (`openmoat mcp proxy`) keeps what a wrapped server's tool returned, for every agent, under the session of the hook's decision for that call, and the Cline CLI's `PostToolUse` file (OpenMoat's script, which `openmoat init` now writes beside `PreToolUse`) reports what each call returned. A later call of the session that uses a host, path or command that untrusted output named needs review (`injected-input`); after an upgrade, `openmoat status` shows Cline's hook out of date until `openmoat init` adds the file ([#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- Writes to the agents' system-wide settings, which outrank the user's and can turn every hook off (Claude Code's managed settings folder, Gemini CLI's system settings, Codex's `/etc/codex`), are denied (`managed-settings`, hook only) ([#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- Package-manager and build configuration that runs code on the next install, script or build (`.npmrc`, `.yarnrc`, `.yarnrc.yml`, `.cargo/config.toml`, `.cargo/config`, `bunfig.toml`) needs review in a clean session too (`runs-later`), and so do pip's and uv's user configuration and `~/.bunfig.toml` (`persistence`) ([#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- `openmoat doctor` and `openmoat status` name the current repository's git configuration that makes git run a program (`core.fsmonitor`, a hooks path, a pager or editor, diff and merge drivers, filters, a credential helper command, `!` aliases, includes), which the agent's own background git runs before any hook; `status --format json` lists them as `repo_git_config` ([#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- An agent's write to a virtual environment's interpreter and scripts (`.venv/bin/`, `venv/bin/`), which the editor's Python extension starts outside any sandbox, or to a dev container's configuration (`.devcontainer/`, `.devcontainer.json`), whose `initializeCommand` runs on this machine, needs review (`runs-later`) ([#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- Injected input (ADR-057) is linked beyond exact matches where the rewrite is the same request (ADR-063 §4): a host in other case or with a trailing dot, an IPv4 address in any numeric form the URL Standard reads (`3405803783` is `203.0.113.7`), an uncompressed IPv6 address, and a subdomain of a host a URL named (`collect.evil.example` after `https://evil.example/…`) unless the policy allows the parent. A planted command run through `sudo`, `env`, `nohup` or `sh -c`, or after `VAR=value`, is linked as before; `rm -r -f x` is still not `rm -rf x`. URLs with a bracketed IPv6 host are now kept too ([#755](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/755)).

### Added
- When OpenMoat denies a call or holds it for review, the agent and you now read what was stopped and what you can do, in every agent: the rule's title, the call (secrets masked), the line earlier releases sent, why the rule exists, one next step for you, and `openmoat why --last` with the rule's page (ADR-059). Rules without a title, and OpenMoat's own checks, keep the one line ([#751](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/751)).
- Policy rules can say `title:` and `why:`; every deny and review rule of the default policy has both, and `openmoat policy upgrade` adds them to your policy. `openmoat policy lint` names the deny and review rules without a title ([#751](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/751)).
- `openmoat watch` follows your agents' calls as they happen, one line each, in a second terminal pane: what ran, what was denied and what waits for you; `--host`, `--only deny|review`, `--session`, `--no-follow`. It reads the audit log only ([#751](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/751)).
- `openmoat why` explains one decision (`--last`, an event id or a time): the rules that decided it and where they are in your policy, how your policy decides each part of the call now, the session's context, and how to change it; `--format json` ([#751](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/751)).
- Collection before a send is recorded (ADR-061, proposed): each session's reads outside the project and the files packed from them are folded from the audit log, a later send of data carries `@staging-egress <host>`, and `openmoat report` counts these sends per session and per active hour. No default rule names the atom, so no decision changes; a policy can add one, and the new optional `staging:` section sets the thresholds (`outside_reads`, default 25; `outside_folders`, default 2) and `ignore` paths ([#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).

### Changed
