- **Breaking** `openmoat-core`: `Decision` has a new public field `notice` (`Option<Box<Notice>>`, never serialised; set it to `None` where you build a decision yourself, or use `Decision::new`), `RuleGroup` has the new public fields `title` and `why`, and `Notice`, `TITLE_MAX_CHARS`, `WHY_MAX_CHARS`, `Policy::own_group`, `Policy::untitled` and `lint::untitled` are new. The policy format gains the optional `title:` and `why:` per rule; an older OpenMoat rejects a policy that uses them. `openmoat-hosts` adds `rule_page` and `RULE_PAGE`, and `reason_line` renders a decision's notice as the block message; `openmoat-audit` adds `Store::after`, `Store::between` and `Store::latest_held` ([#751](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/751)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-hosts`: `HostSpec` has a new public field `bundled` (`&'static [Bundle]`), and the new public type `Bundle` names an editor's extensions folder and the extension that bundles a host's program; Claude Code's `HostSpec` lists the VS Code extension's folders, and Cursor's hooks include `beforeTabFileRead` ([#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-core`: `Policy` has a new public field `staging` (`Option<StagingSettings>`; set it to `None` where you build a policy yourself, or use `Policy::parse`), and `StagingSettings`, `Staging`, `STAGING_ATOM`, `DEFAULT_OUTSIDE_READS`, `DEFAULT_OUTSIDE_FOLDERS`, `CompiledPolicy::collection` and `CompiledPolicy::with_staging` are new ([#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- Every command shows a verdict with the same mark: `✓ allow`, `→ defer`, `? review`, `✕ deny` (`policy check`, `demo`, `show`, `replay` and `watch`, which showed `·` for a defer). Each mark is one column wide, so `demo`'s table lines up. `audit verify`, `audit anchor` and `provenance --verify` mark a failure `✗`, as `status` and `doctor` do. A script that matched the earlier `⛔`, `❓` or `✔` should match the verdict word instead ([#761](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/761)).
- A call that uses what a page or tool result planted gets one `injected-input` reason per source, naming each part once: "the command `…`, the host `…` and the path `…` came from the page fetched from …", where it was one reason per part, each repeating the source ([#761](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/761)).

### Fixed
- The Cline CLI's npm package is `cline` (it was named as `@cline/cli`, Cline's separate experimental CLI, in its host notes and the weekly latest-agents check, which installed the wrong package); OpenMoat also recognises the compiled Cline binary that the `cline` wrapper starts (`node_modules/cline/bin/.cline`) as Cline, not only the wrapper ([#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).
- `openmoat policy upgrade` could refuse to upgrade a policy started from an older default with an edit near the top of a rule list ("the upgraded policy does not lint"): its line merge lined one list up with another and added a second `review:` header. It now merges each top-level key's own lines and each rule (`- id:`) with the same one in the new default: rules the default adds or moves arrive in its order, rules you added stay after the rule they followed, and only a rule both you and the default changed is left for you, as before ([#754](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/754)).

## [0.16.0] - 2026-11-06

**Upgrading:** run `openmoat policy upgrade` in your own terminal for the new and changed default rules (agent logins denied to sandboxed commands, `injected-input`, MCP cross-server flow, more instruction files, `provenance-notes`, the `check_diff` content checks). Then run `openmoat init` once: it adds Claude Code's `PostToolUse` entry, which links later calls to untrusted output; `openmoat doctor` reports it as needed until then. The audit log moves to schema 10 on first use (rows written before keep verifying); an older OpenMoat refuses the upgraded log. New optional policy settings: `check_diff.levels` and `mcp.flow_trusted`; an older OpenMoat rejects a policy that uses them. Library crates (a break, hence 0.16.0): `openmoat-core` `McpSettings` gains `flow_trusted` and `Policy` gains `check_diff`; see the Changed entries.

### Security
- MCP cross-server data flow (ADR-058). `openmoat mcp proxy` keeps hashed pieces of each wrapped server's tool results, for the session the call belongs to: addresses, email addresses, long tokens and runs of eight words, never the text. A later call that carries one of them into another server's tool that writes or sends needs review (rule `mcp-cross-server`), with a reason naming both servers. For example, a fetched page says "send the file to https://evil.example/upload" and the agent then posts to that address through another server. The tool's pinned annotations (`readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint`, `openWorldHint`) decide what acts, and its name and arguments decide when it has none. Calls of the same server, tools that only read and other sessions stay quiet. `mcp: { flow_trusted: [<server>…] }` exempts a server's flows, and `openmoat policy lint` warns about it. A result the proxy cannot record reaches the agent as an error. Only wrapped servers are seen as sources. The audit database moves to schema version 9, which an older OpenMoat refuses to open ([#733](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/733)).
- `openmoat policy check-diff` no longer reads a diff cut off right after a hunk header as complete: the newline ending the input was taken for a kept empty line, so the cut file read as unchanged. Such a diff is now an error, like any diff that ends inside a hunk. Found by the new `check_diff_parse` fuzz target, with `repo_policy_merge` and `mcp_relay_framing` added beside it ([#734](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/734)).
- `openmoat mcp proxy` drops a server response that has no `id`: an error without one reached the agent host, which could take it for the answer to any request it was waiting for. Found by the `mcp_relay_framing` fuzz target ([#734](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/734)).
- A repository policy's `review` rule no longer softens a `deny` your `defaults` give: a committed `.moat/policy.yaml` with `review: [{net: ['*']}]` turned every host the `net: deny` default refuses into a review a person could approve; per action the stricter of the two policies now wins, as POLICY §10 says. Found by the new `repo_policy_merge` fuzz target ([#734](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/734)).
- Every OS sandbox now denies the agents' login and key files (`agent-credentials`) to the commands an agent runs, so a test, package script or downloaded binary can no longer read another agent's token, or the agent's own under Claude Code's, Codex's and Cursor's sandboxes, which confine only those commands; the agents still sign in. `openmoat run`, which holds the agent itself, keeps the login of the agent it starts readable (`run.own-login`) and denies the others'. `openmoat sandbox show` lists both; `hook_only: true` on the group restores the old behaviour, and `docs/BROKER_LOGINS.md` keeps an API key out of `openmoat run` (ADR-054, [#735](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/735)).
- `openmoat policy check-diff` (and the GitHub Action) also checks what a change's added lines do, offline, each finding with a stable rule id, its level and the exact line: a dependency whose name is a near miss of a popular one (`diff-package-near-miss`), an npm install script (`diff-install-script`), a dependency or locked package from git, plain http or another host than its registry (`diff-package-source`), a lockfile hash changed or removed while the version stays (`diff-lock-integrity`, deny); in GitHub workflows, `pull_request_target` with a checkout of the pull request's code (`diff-ci-pr-target`, deny), write permissions (`diff-ci-permissions`), `uses:` without a full commit SHA (`diff-ci-unpinned`), an author's text such as an issue title expanded into a script (`diff-ci-injection`, deny) and a printed secret (`diff-ci-secret-out`, deny); `curl … | sh` (`diff-pipe-to-shell`), decoded text run as code (`diff-decode-exec`, deny), a `.pth` file with an `import` line (`diff-python-startup`, deny), a Dockerfile `ADD` of a URL without `--checksum` (`diff-docker-remote-add`), a long base64 blob in source code (`diff-encoded-blob`), a private key (`diff-private-key`, deny; review in a test fixture or marked fake), a real-looking `.env` value (`diff-env-secret`), and one finding per new host that the policy's network allow list does not name (`diff-new-host`). The policy's new `check_diff.levels` sets any of them to `deny`, `review` or `off` (docs/MERGE_CHECK.md, ADR-055) ([#736](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/736)).
- Prompt-injection data flow (ADR-057). When Claude Code reports what a call returned (a new `PostToolUse` hook entry), and the output is untrusted (a fetched page, web search results, an MCP result, `gh issue view`, a `curl` of an unlisted host), OpenMoat keeps keyed fingerprints of its URL hosts, host-like words, outside paths and code-span commands, never the text. A later shell command or write that uses one of them, such as `curl` to the host an issue body named, needs review (`injected-input`), and the reason names the source. Anything the policy allows on its own stays allowed (builds, project files, the registries, `git push`), and fetches and searches stay the agent's own prompt. Run `openmoat init` once to add the hook entry; `openmoat doctor` reports it as needed until then. Other agents do not report output yet, as each HOST.md says ([#737](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/737)).
- After the session reads untrusted content, writes to `.agents/skills/**`, `.windsurfrules`, `.windsurf/rules/**` and `.github/instructions/**` (instruction and skills files the next agent session obeys) need review, as `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` already did. In a clean session they stay ordinary edits ([#737](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/737)).
- Agents cannot write OpenMoat's provenance record. `openmoat provenance --write-notes` from an agent is denied (`kernel-self`). An agent's `git notes` on the `openmoat` notes ref, `git update-ref` of `refs/notes/openmoat` and `git update-ref --stdin` need review (new `provenance-notes` group). Other notes refs and reading history are unchanged ([#738](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/738)).

### Added
- `openmoat provenance` shows which files of your commits an agent session wrote, from this machine's audit log, and where one recorded write made the whole change, which added lines. For each file it names the agent, the session and the time, and says whether the session had read web content, an MCP result or a file such a session wrote before writing. `--write-notes` (person-only) keeps a versioned JSON note per commit on `refs/notes/openmoat`; share them with `git push origin refs/notes/openmoat`. `--verify` checks each note's digest, audit head and cited events against the log, and attributes the commit again. `openmoat policy check-diff` and the merge check Action show the notes as review-level lines (`--provenance review|info|off`, Action input `provenance`); they never change the exit code. A missing note proves nothing (docs/PROVENANCE.md, ADR-056) ([#738](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/738)).

### Changed
- `openmoat doctor`, `openmoat status` and `openmoat init` print one layout: what needs you first, each with its fix after `→`, one line per agent, then OpenMoat's own state, with the same marks everywhere (`✔ ! ✗ · –`), paths as `~/…`, and colour only on a terminal without `NO_COLOR`. Each check with its paths, each agent's known gaps, the hook entries `init` writes and its backups are shown with `--verbose` (`openmoat init --verbose` is new). `--format json` output and exit codes are unchanged ([#732](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/732)).
- `openmoat init` no longer asks about an agent whose files already have everything (a yes would change nothing), and ends with the agents protected and only the steps this run leaves to you, such as trusting the changed hook in Codex ([#732](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/732)).
- `openmoat policy upgrade` groups what the new default changes (new protections, fewer interruptions, the `ask` rename, comment updates counted) instead of printing the whole diff; `d` at the question, which stays `[y/N/d]` with no as the default, or the new `--diff` shows the diff. After applying it writes the agents' sandboxes as `openmoat sandbox sync` does, after the same checks ([#732](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/732)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-core`: `McpSettings` has a new public field `flow_trusted` (start from `McpSettings::default()` where you build one yourself). Added: the `mcp_flow` module (`ToolEffect`, `ToolHints`, `pieces`, `cross_server`, `FLOW_RULE`) and `Policy::mcp_flow_trusted`. `openmoat-hosts`: added `mcp_tool_acts`. `openmoat-audit`: added `Store::add_mcp_flow`, `Store::mcp_flow_sources`, `MCP_FLOW_MAX_AGE_MS` and `MCP_FLOW_SESSION_ROWS`, schema version 9 ([#733](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/733)).
- The policy compiler writes each brace alternative of a file pattern as a pattern of its own (`~/.codex/{auth.json,.credentials.json}` reaches Claude Code's `denyRead` and Codex's profile as two paths); `openmoat_core::ir::brace_alternatives`, `EvalContext::spellings` and `openmoat_hosts::Host::by_program` are new public functions ([#735](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/735)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-core`: `Policy` has a new public field `check_diff` (`Option<diffscan::Settings>`), and the new public module `diffscan` holds the content checks of `openmoat policy check-diff` (`scan`, `new_hosts`, `RULES`, `Block`, `Change`, `Hit`, `Level`, `Settings`). The policy format gains the optional `check_diff:` section; an older OpenMoat rejects a policy that uses it ([#736](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/736)).

## [0.15.0] - 2026-11-05

**Upgrading:** run `openmoat policy upgrade` in your own terminal for the changed default rules (card ids in URLs, queries and searches are no longer denied; writes to `.githooks`-style folders and Cline's workspace hooks need review; `git config <name>` reads are allowed; an agent running `openmoat guard` itself is denied). Codex: run `openmoat init` again and trust OpenMoat's hook in Codex's `/hooks`, because the hook now also covers `read_mcp_resource` and `view_image`. The audit log upgrade to schema 8 no longer copies the log, so a large log no longer blocks calls after upgrading from 0.13. Library crates (a break, hence 0.15.0): `openmoat-hosts` gains the public field `HookRequest::sandboxed` and new `HostSpec` fields.

### Security
- Codex and Cursor no longer take the tool's working directory, which the model chooses, as the project: a command run from outside the session's project (Codex's `workdir`, a Cursor `cwd` outside every workspace root) is decided from the session's directory as running there, so a write from `~/Library/LaunchAgents` is checked as that path ([#726](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/726)).
- Codex: a command that starts a shell, REPL, editor or login with no script or command of its own (`bash`, `python3`, `node`, `sqlite3 db`, `ssh host`, …) needs review, since Codex types later input into it with `write_stdin`, which runs no hook; `bash x.sh`, `python3 x.py` and `ssh host cmd` are decided as before ([#726](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/726)).
- Codex: `read_mcp_resource` and `view_image` are hooked and checked as the resource or image they read; trust OpenMoat's hook again in Codex's `/hooks` after `openmoat init`, since its matcher changed ([#726](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/726)).
- Hermes Agent: a review's `rule_key` names the call, so Hermes's "always" approves that call again and never another one held for the same reason ([#726](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/726)).
- Hermes Agent: a relative file path is checked against the directory Hermes started in instead of always needing review; one that climbs out (`..`) and a `terminal` call that moves the session's shell outside that directory need review, and the message names the file and whether it is read or written ([#726](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/726)).
- Gemini CLI: `grep_search` and `glob` without a `dir_path`, and `read_many_files`, are checked over the working directory and the `context.includeDirectories` of the user and project settings instead of always asking ([#726](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/726)).
- Cursor: a command Cursor runs in its sandbox (`sandbox: true`) that no rule decides is allowed, as Cursor would run it, while OpenMoat's `sandbox.json` is installed; it no longer asks ([#726](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/726)).
- Cline: the payload limit counts Cline's two copies of a call's arguments once, so a file is refused at the same size as on other agents, with a message that says to write it in parts ([#726](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/726)).
- `openmoat init` says when it moves a person's own whole-file hook (Cline's `PreToolUse`) aside, and `init` and `uninstall` keep a host file's permission bits instead of making it owner-only ([#726](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/726)).
- `INSTALL.md` names goose's real hook file, `~/.agents/plugins/openmoat/hooks/hooks.json` ([#726](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/726)).
- Writes to the git hook folders a repository's `core.hooksPath` usually names (`.githooks/`, `.git-hooks/`, `githooks/`, `git-hooks/`, `scripts/git-hooks/` included, and the folders themselves) need review (`runs-later`) and are in the session taint list; before, a write there was allowed by `project-fs`, though git runs those files on the next commit or push. Folders merely named `hooks` (React hooks, docs) stay ordinary writes ([#727](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/727)).
- Writes to Cline's workspace hooks (`.clinerules/hooks/`, `.cline/hooks/`, every file of which Cline runs on each tool call) and renames onto `.clinerules`, `.cline` or their `hooks` folders need review (`runs-later`), as other agents' project hook folders do; before, `project-fs` allowed them. Rules files in `.clinerules/` stay ordinary writes ([#727](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/727)).
- A quote or a backslash now ends a variable's name, as in the shell: `d=~/.ssh/id; cat "$d"_rsa` (also `$d'_rsa'`, `$d"_rsa"`, `$d\_rsa`) is a read of `~/.ssh/id_rsa` and denied by `secrets-paths`, and `echo "$GITHUB_TOKEN"x` reads `GITHUB_TOKEN` (`env-secrets`); before, they were read as the unknown variables `d_rsa` and `GITHUB_TOKENx` and deferred ([#727](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/727)).
- An editor (`vi`, `vim`, `nvim`, `nano`, `emacs`, `code`) saves the file it opens, so its file operands are decided as writes too: `vim /usr/local/bin/openmoat` and `nano ~/.moat/policy.yaml` are denied by `kernel-self` and `nvim ~/.zshrc` by `shell-rc`, as writing them is; an editor on a project file keeps its verdict ([#727](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/727)).
- The audit log now masks more passwords and keys before it stores a command: `mysql -p<password>`, `PGPASSWORD=`, `MYSQL_PWD=` and other environment names ending in `PASSWORD`, `_KEY`, `_SECRET`, `_TOKEN` or `_AUTH`, `sshpass -p`, `docker login -p`, `redis-cli -a`, `openssl -k` and `-pass pass:`, `zip -P`, `htpasswd -b`, `Cookie:` and `Set-Cookie:` values, `aws configure set aws_secret_access_key`, Slack and Discord webhook URLs, and a key given as the user part of a URL (a Sentry DSN). The same flags of other programs (`cargo test -p`, `ssh -p`, `mkdir -p`) keep their values ([#728](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/728)).
- An agent can no longer run `openmoat guard` or `openmoat mcp proxy` from its own shell tool (`kernel-self`): only a host runs them, so an agent cannot write audit rows, MCP tickets or a session's proxy credential for payloads it made up. `--help` still works ([#728](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/728)).
- `openmoat allow --last` and `openmoat policy upgrade` (and `--undo`) refuse to re-pin when a pinned file or the lock changed while they waited for the answer, instead of pinning the change unseen ([#728](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/728)).
- The guard works out again whether an agent's OS sandbox is in place when Claude Code's `blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories` is turned off, so code a session wrote is held for review at once rather than after the next re-pin ([#728](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/728)).
- The `openmoat run` Seatbelt profile also denies hard links (`file-link`) to every path it denies reading or writing, so a sandboxed command cannot link `~/.moat/proxy.key` or `audit.db` into the project ([#728](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/728)).

### Added
- `@openmoat/sdk` is on npm (`npm install @openmoat/sdk`): each release now publishes it from the release workflow through npm's trusted publishing, with provenance, after the SDK's conformance tests pass ([#730](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/730)).

### Changed
- **Breaking** `openmoat-hosts`: `HookRequest` has a new public field `sandboxed` (set it to `false` where you build a request yourself, or start from a parsed one). Added: `HookRequest::from_session_dir`, `HookRequest::searches_workspace`, `HookRequest::with_workspace_dirs`, `Host::render_response_for` (use it instead of `render_response` when you have the request), the `SessionDir` enum and the `HostSpec` fields `session_dir`, `workspace_dirs` and `payload_copies` ([#726](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/726)).
- Cline CLI: `openmoat status` and `doctor` print Cline's limits on a line of their own (`cline --yolo` runs no hooks; another hook or plugin can rewrite a call after OpenMoat checked it; Cline runs the call when OpenMoat's script cannot start or is killed after 120 s), and name every other `PreToolUse` file in Cline's hook folders and every Cline plugin they find; `status --format json` lists them under `other_hooks` and gives each agent's `limit` ([#729](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/729)).
- Linux: plain `openmoat run` says on standard error when it starts that commands the agent starts can still read and write the project's `.env`, create `.envrc` and write `.moat` and `.git/hooks`, and that `openmoat run --isolate` closes this (naming bubblewrap when it is missing); `openmoat doctor` warns about the same, and `status`, `doctor` and `init` name `openmoat run --isolate` for an agent without a sandbox of its own ([#729](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/729)).
- Docs: README "Limits" and THREAT_MODEL say plainly that agent login files are denied to the agent's own tool calls but stay readable to programs an allowed command runs, inside every OS sandbox too; ADR-054 (proposed) weighs the options and plans denying them in the host sandboxes that confine only the agent's commands ([#729](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/729)).
- README: the features list says what the secret checks do instead of "Nothing sensitive leaves": recognised secrets and card numbers in a call are denied (known token formats sent to the wrong host, encoded secrets after a secret was read, payment card numbers), and file contents and encoded data are not read; secret files are denied to the agent's tool calls, and the OS sandboxes confine code within what each can deny ([#729](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/729)).

### Fixed
- `card-egress` no longer denies ordinary ids: a contiguous 13 to 19 digit number counts as a card only in a card's context (the value of a field named like a card's, `card`, `cardNumber`, `cc`, `pan`, `card[number]`, …, or sent beside a security code or expiry such as `cvv` or `exp`), so a GitHub repository or job id in an API path, an order id or a number in a query or a search is no longer denied. Card numbers in printed groups (`4000 1234 5678 9017`) count wherever they are (ADR-052, amended) ([#727](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/727)).
- `git config user.name`, `git config user.email` and other `git config <section>.<key>` reads with nothing after the name (also after one scope option such as `--global`) are allowed like `git config --get`; a value after the name, or a subcommand such as `edit`, still needs review ([#727](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/727)).
- `for n in 1 2; do sed -n "$n"p f; done` and `sed -n $n'p'` are read as the shell reads them (`${n}p`) and allowed like `sed -n "${n}p"`, instead of needing review ([#727](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/727)).
- The first hook call after upgrading from 0.13 no longer copies the whole audit log to accept the `review` verdict: the schema step now widens its check in place, in milliseconds at any size. On a large log the copy ran past the guard's 30 s budget, was rolled back and started again on the next call, so every call was denied ([#728](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/728)).
- Loading the policy on each hook call is faster: the check for the old `ask` name no longer parses the default policy a second time, and merging a repository policy, a pack or `openmoat allow` rules no longer parses every pattern of the policy again (about 14 ms less per call with a repository policy) ([#728](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/728)).
- When a call's provenance, MCP ticket or `openmoat run` link cannot be written after its row, the audit log now also records the deny the host was given, so the log's last word on the call matches the answer ([#728](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/728)).
- A panic while the guard decides is denied in the format of the payload's event (a `ConfigChange` is blocked as one), not always as a tool call ([#728](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/728)).
- goose: `openmoat status` and `doctor` report goose not protected in a directory whose `.agents/plugins/openmoat` replaces OpenMoat's plugin (any hook file but a byte-for-byte copy of OpenMoat's), name the directory to remove, and warn about the hooks of the project's other plugins; they used to report goose protected there ([#729](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/729)).

## [0.14.0] - 2026-11-05

**Upgrading:** the `ask` verdict is now called `review`. Your policy keeps working; run `openmoat policy upgrade` in your own terminal to rename `ask:` and `ask` to `review` (lint warns until you do) and to get the new default rule `card-egress`, which denies a payment card number sent off the machine. JSON output says `"review"`, and the `asked` / `top_asks` keys are now `reviewed` / `top_reviews`. Audit logs written by older releases still read and verify. New agents: goose and the Cline CLI; run `openmoat init` once to protect them. Library crates: `openmoat-core`, `openmoat-hosts` and `openmoat-sdk` rename `Verdict::Ask` to `Verdict::Review` and the items named after it, and `Host` gains `Cline` and `Goose`.

### Security
- A payment card number sent off the machine is denied (`card-egress`, ADR-052): 13 to 19 digits with a card brand's prefix and length that pass the Luhn check, contiguous or in printed groups, in a URL a fetch, network or MCP tool opens or names, a web search query, or the arguments of a command that connects to a host (`curl -d`, `-H`, `--json`, a query, a pipe into `curl`), allowed hosts included. Sends to this machine (`localhost`, `127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`) and the test numbers payment processors publish are left out; file contents and encoded data are not read. Ten or more distinct mail addresses in one send become the atom `@pii-egress emails <host>`, which no default rule names, so they change no decision unless a policy adds a rule. A policy without a `@pii-egress` rule decides as before; `openmoat policy upgrade` adds `card-egress` ([#719](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/719)).

### Added
- goose support (#263): `openmoat init` writes OpenMoat's goose plugin, `~/.agents/plugins/openmoat/hooks/hooks.json`, with one `PreToolUse` entry for every tool that sets `on_failure: block`, so a hook that cannot start, crashes or times out blocks the call. `shell`, `write`, `edit`, `tree`, `analyze`, `read_image` and extension tools are checked; goose cannot prompt, so a review is a block approved with `openmoat allow --last`; Code Mode's `execute_javascript` needs review, since its tool calls skip the hook. MCP extensions in `config.yaml` are pinned, `disabledPlugins` naming `openmoat` reports goose as not protected, and the default policy keeps the agent from changing goose's configuration and any `.agents/plugins` directory ([#715](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/715)).
- The Cline CLI (`cline`, npm `@cline/cli`) is supported: `openmoat init --hosts cline` writes OpenMoat's `PreToolUse` hook script to `~/.cline/hooks` (`CLINE_DIR` moves it), which Cline runs before every tool call. `run_commands` (every command shape Cline accepts), `read_files`, `editor`, `apply_patch`, `search_codebase`, `fetch_web_content` and MCP tools are checked, and any other tool by what its arguments name; the servers in Cline's `cline_mcp_settings.json` are pinned. Cline runs a call whose hook fails, so the script itself blocks the call when `openmoat` is missing or does not answer. Cline's hooks cannot prompt, so a review is a deny you approve with `openmoat allow --last`. The default policy's `kernel-self` now protects Cline's hook and plugin directories (and `~/Documents/Cline/Hooks`), `agent-credentials` its stored provider keys and connector credentials, and session taint its `.cline` and `.clinerules` files. The Cline VS Code extension, `--yolo` runs and native Windows are not covered ([#716](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/716)).
- `openmoat policy check-diff` checks a change before it is merged: from `--base` to `--head`, or a unified diff on stdin. Every file the change adds, edits, deletes or renames is decided by the policy's own rules as a write from untrusted content, so CI workflows, git hooks, build and package files and agent settings are reported for `review` and what the policy denies an agent (`.claude/settings.json`, `.env`, `.moat/`) as `deny`; added lines holding a known token format, symbolic links, submodules and paths outside the repository are reported too. Paths are compared without letter case and in their Windows spelling. With `--base` the policy files (`--policy` and the repository's `.moat/policy.yaml`) are read as the base revision has them, so a change cannot loosen its own check, and a change to one is reported. Exit 0 clean, 2 deny, 3 review; `--format text`, `json` or `github` (workflow annotations, the default under GitHub Actions). A composite `action.yml` at the repository root runs it on pull requests with a pinned release whose sha256 and build attestation it checks (`docs/MERGE_CHECK.md`, ADR-050) ([#717](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/717)).
- `openmoat audit evidence [--since …] [--until …] [--host …] [--samples N] [--format json]` reports the audit log for a time window as evidence per control of OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications 2025, the SOC 2 Trust Services Criteria (CC6 to CC8) and ISO/IEC 27001:2022 Annex A: which rules fired, decisions by verdict, sample events as stored (redacted), the hash chain and the window's newest hash, and the policy hash, lock and each agent's protection level when the report is made. The rule → control map is data in the CLI, tested against the default policy; a default rule the policy in force lacks is marked as unable to fire. It is evidence, not a certification (`docs/COMPLIANCE.md`, ADR-051) ([#718](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/718)).

### Changed
- **Breaking** `openmoat-hosts`: `Host` gains `Goose`, so an exhaustive `match` on `Host` needs the new arm ([#715](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/715)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-hosts`: `Host` gains `Cline`, `HookLayout` gains `Script` (a hook script OpenMoat owns whole), and `OwnedFile` gains a `syntax` field (`JavaScript` or `PosixShell`); an exhaustive `match` on these enums needs the new arms, and code that builds an `OwnedFile` sets `syntax` ([#716](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/716)).
- **Breaking** `openmoat-core`, `openmoat-hosts` and `openmoat-sdk`: the `ask` verdict is `review` (ADR-053). `Verdict::Ask` is `Verdict::Review` (and `Verdict::as_str` gives `"review"`), `Policy::ask`/`repo_ask` and `RepoPolicy::ask` are `review`/`repo_review`, `PolicyError` gains `OldAndNewName`, `Policy` gains `old_names` (`OldNames`), `openmoat_hosts::Asks` and `HostSpec::asks` are `Reviews` and `HostSpec::reviews` (with `on_no_prompt` and `defer_is_review_on_no_prompt`), `HookEvent::PreToolUseNoAsk` is `PreToolUseNoPrompt`, `bench::Answer::Ask` is `Answer::Review`, `openmoat_audit::Summary::asked` is `reviewed` and `reviews_per_active_hour_centi`; the SDK's `Verdict::Ask` is `Verdict::Review`. Serialised verdicts are `"review"`; `"ask"` is still read. No deprecated aliases are kept ([#721](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/721)).
- The verdict `ask` is now called `review`, everywhere OpenMoat shows or stores it: the policy's `review:` rule list and `review` default, `openmoat status`, `doctor`, `show`, `report`, `audit` and MoatBench output (`reviewed` counts, `top reviews`, `❓ review`), `--format json` (`"verdict": "review"`, and the keys `reviewed` and `top_reviews` where they said `asked` and `top_asks`), the reason line (`moat: review [rule]`), the SDKs and the docs. Nothing else changes: the same calls need a person's approval, exit code 3 still means an unresolved review, and every agent still receives its own protocol's word (`"ask"`). Upgrading: a policy that says `ask:` or `ask` keeps working as before and `openmoat policy lint` warns about it; run `openmoat policy upgrade`, which shows the rename in its diff and asks before writing. A policy with both `ask:` and `review:` is refused, with a message saying which rules to move. Audit rows written before keep `ask` (the hash chain covers them) and read as `review`; the audit database moves to schema version 8, which an older OpenMoat refuses to open ([#721](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/721)).

## [0.13.0] - 2026-11-03

**Upgrading:** wrapped MCP servers (`openmoat mcp wrap`, `openmoat init --mcp-proxy`) now start only from a definition a person accepted: a wrapped server whose definition is new or changed does not start until you run `openmoat mcp accept <server>` in your own terminal, and a wrapped server does not start inside `openmoat run`. Re-wrapping with `openmoat mcp wrap` turns the sandbox on for servers on macOS 13 or later and Linux; `--no-sandbox` keeps one unsandboxed. Building from source needs Rust 1.99. Library crates: `openmoat-hosts` adds `McpSpec::auto_start` (`McpAutoStart`).

### Security
- `openmoat mcp proxy` now checks its own command line against the MCP server definitions a person accepted (the pin `openmoat mcp accept` writes) before it starts anything: a new or changed wrapped server never runs its command; the agent gets an error naming `openmoat mcp accept <server>`, and the refusal is recorded (rule `mcp-server-changed`, host `mcp-proxy`). A drifted lock or an unreadable policy starts no wrapped server ([#712](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/712)).
- `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` warn about MCP servers that start outside OpenMoat: stdio servers that are not wrapped, wrapped ones without the sandbox, and project servers Claude Code starts without asking (`enableAllProjectMcpServers`, `enabledMcpjsonServers`) ([#712](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/712)).

### Changed
- `openmoat mcp wrap` and `openmoat init --mcp-proxy` start servers under `openmoat run`'s sandbox by default wherever it can sandbox (macOS 13 or later, Linux); `--no-sandbox` (`--mcp-no-sandbox` for `init`) opts out, and wrapping an already wrapped server keeps its choice unless a flag is given ([#712](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/712)).
- `openmoat mcp proxy` started by hand, or by a client whose configuration OpenMoat does not read, no longer starts the server ([#712](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/712)).
- Building OpenMoat from source (`cargo install openmoat`, or a clone) now needs Rust 1.99 or later (was 1.95); the release binaries and Homebrew are unaffected ([#713](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/713)).

### Fixed
- A guard that gives up after its 30 s budget now waits up to 2 s (was 200 ms) for its line in `~/.moat/guard-hangs.log` before it exits, so `openmoat doctor` no longer misses hangs when several guards give up at once on a slow disk; the deny still reaches every agent well before its hook timeout ([#708](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/708)).

## [0.12.1] - 2026-10-31

**Upgrading:** Codex users: OpenMoat did not check Codex's tool calls in 0.12.0 and earlier with current Codex releases (the hook ran `openmoat` without its arguments and every call went through). After upgrading, run `openmoat init` once in your own terminal, then open Codex, run `/hooks` and trust the OpenMoat hook; `openmoat status` says when it is not trusted yet. Nothing else changes.

### Security
- Codex hooks now invoke `guard --host codex` in one command string instead of ignored `args`, with safe quoting on POSIX and Windows. Existing hooks are repaired by `init`, `policy upgrade` and `sandbox sync`; status and doctor report broken or untrusted hooks, and bare OpenMoat denies hook input ([#705](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/705)).

### Fixed
- Codex: an allowed call is answered with no output instead of `permissionDecision: allow`, which Codex accepts only together with `updatedInput` and otherwise reports as a failed hook ([#706](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/706)).

## [0.12.0] - 2026-10-29

**Upgrading:** run `openmoat init` once in your own terminal, so Claude Code gets the `SessionStart` hook that gives each session its credential for `openmoat proxy`, then `openmoat policy upgrade` to take the new default rules (`encoded-egress`, the proxy key and certificate authority under `kernel-self`, the `destructive-recoverable` ask group, the `openmoat-readonly` allow group and the new `env-poison` names), and restart your agents. The audit log moves to schema 7 the first time the new `openmoat` writes to it; an older `openmoat` then refuses to open it. Library crates: `openmoat-hosts` adds `HookRequest.edits`, `HookRequest.mcp_arguments` and `Replacement`; `openmoat-core` adds `Action::McpTool.opens`; `openmoat-sdk` and `@openmoat/sdk` ship for the first time (ADR-046).

### Security
- `openmoat mcp proxy` is now an MCP gateway (ADR-045): a call OpenMoat refuses never reaches the server, and every call it passes is tied to the agent session that made it. A `tools/call` passes only when the policy does not deny it as the server receives it (the paths and URLs its arguments name, under the name `mcp__<server>__<tool>` whatever the agent calls the tool) and when the hook decided exactly that call: the same tool and arguments, within the hour, each decision used once. The proxy's audit row (host `mcp-proxy`) carries the hook's session and call id, so the session's taint and the person's answer to an `ask` apply. A call no hook decided is refused with rule `mcp-unattributed` ([#687](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/687)).
- The proxy no longer passes on bytes it did not check: every message it sends is the one it read, written again by itself, so a key given twice, a `\r` inside a line or a line separator inside a string can no longer carry a message the proxy did not check to the server, or an unfiltered tool listing to the agent. A line from the server it cannot read is dropped instead of passed on. Batches inside batches, messages that are both a request and a response, ids that are not strings or numbers, reused ids and a method spelled in another case are refused ([#687](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/687)).
- A response from the server reaches the agent only when it answers a request the agent sent, so a server can no longer answer a call the proxy refused or send a tool listing nobody asked for ([#687](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/687)).
- When a server says its tools changed (`notifications/tools/list_changed`), the proxy reads them again itself and holds each changed tool, even if the agent keeps its old list. Under `mcp.trust_first_sight`, a call made before the server's first listing is refused ([#687](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/687)).
- `resources/read` through the proxy is decided on its URI (a `file:` URI as the file it names, an `https:` one as the host), and `prompts/get` and the server's own requests to the agent (`sampling/createMessage`, `elicitation/create`) are recorded ([#687](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/687)).
- The audit database is at schema version 6: it holds the MCP calls the hook let through until the proxy uses each or it is an hour old. An older database is upgraded when OpenMoat first writes to it ([#687](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/687)).
- A server wrapped with `openmoat mcp wrap` now answers only calls an agent's OpenMoat hook decided; a script or tool that used a wrapped server directly gets error -32001 (`mcp-unattributed`). `openmoat mcp unwrap <server>` takes the proxy out of its path ([#687](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/687)).
- `openmoat proxy` now serves agent sessions only (ADR-044). Claude Code's new `SessionStart` hook gives each session's sandboxed commands a proxy credential of their own (signed, accepted for 24 hours, issued again at every start, resume, `/clear` and compaction), so the proxy records each request under that session, applies its taint (after the session reads a secret, its commands' connections to other hosts are refused) and its share of the connections. A request without a valid credential, from another local program or with a credential edited, borrowed from another session or expired, gets `407` and a `proxy-session` row. The key is `~/.moat/proxy.key`, which the default policy denies to agents (`kernel-self`); replacing it revokes every credential. Run `openmoat init` after upgrading so Claude Code gets the `SessionStart` entry, then start a new session ([#688](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/688)).
- Codex's proxy cannot send a session credential, so `openmoat proxy` now refuses what a Codex started with `HTTP_PROXY` hands on. `sandbox.proxy_serve_without_credential: true` serves requests that carry no credential, with no session, as before (`openmoat policy lint`, `doctor` and `status` warn about it); `openmoat run -- codex` gives each run a credential of its own. `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` say for each host sandbox whether its traffic carries a session ([#688](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/688)).
- A browser MCP call that only opens a page (Chrome DevTools MCP `navigate_page` and `new_page`, Playwright MCP `browser_navigate`, Puppeteer `puppeteer_navigate`, Claude in Chrome `navigate`, Browserbase `navigate`) is decided like `WebFetch`: it defers to the agent's own prompt instead of being denied for every unlisted host. A call that sends more than its URL (a script on the page, launch options, headers, a form) stays denied for unlisted hosts, an IP address or single-label host stays `net`, a `localhost` dev server still defers through `local-net`, and `mcp: { browser_open: net }` keeps the old behaviour. A policy whose `defaults.fetch` or `fetch` rules allow a host now allows browser page opens of it too ([#689](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/689)).
- A URL is judged by every host a client may read it as reaching, including the URL Standard's reading that browsers and `WebFetch` follow: `WebFetch https://evil.com\@github.com/` was decided as `github.com`, which the default policy allows, and is now also `evil.com`; tabs, newlines, `http:/` with one slash and a percent-encoded host no longer hide a metadata address from `cloud-metadata` ([#689](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/689)).
- A `file:` URL in an MCP URL argument is a read of the file it names, so `navigate file:///Users/you/.ssh/id_rsa` is denied by `secrets-paths` instead of deferring; `javascript:`, `data:`, `chrome:` and other URLs that name no network address ask ([#689](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/689)).
- New deny group `encoded-egress`: once the session read secret material, a URL a fetch, network or MCP tool opens that carries 80 or more characters of base64 or hex is denied; run `openmoat policy upgrade` to add it to an existing policy ([#689](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/689)).
- A browser's screenshot, snapshot or trace tool writes the file its path names, so `take_screenshot {filePath: "~/.zshrc"}` is denied by `shell-rc` ([#689](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/689)).
- A brokered secret can now be used over HTTPS without the agent ever holding it, and only for the requests its rules name (ADR-043). With `tls: terminate`, `openmoat run`'s proxy decrypts the agent's tunnel to the secret's host with its own certificate authority, puts the value in, verifies the host's certificate itself, and stops an answer before any brokered value in it. `allow: [GET /repos/**, POST /repos/*/*/issues]` refuses every other request to the host before anything is sent (`proxy-secret-route`), plain HTTP included, along with `..` in any spelling, encoded slashes, `;`, double encoding and method overrides. `openmoat proxy ca init` (person-only, asks first) makes the CA, limited by name constraints to those hosts and pinned in the policy lock; only the commands `openmoat run` starts trust it. `openmoat secret add --tls-terminate --allow '…'` sets both up ([#691](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/691)).
- The default policy's `kernel-self` now denies reading `~/.moat/proxy-ca/`, the CA's key and certificate, and every host sandbox OpenMoat writes keeps it from sandboxed commands; `openmoat policy upgrade` offers the rule ([#691](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/691)).

### Added
- An SDK puts OpenMoat's decisions into your own agent product (docs/SDK.md, ADR-046): `openmoat-sdk` for Rust and `@openmoat/sdk` for JavaScript (`sdk/js`, the engine as a WebAssembly module that imports nothing; no dependencies, no network, no telemetry). Compile a policy once, then decide each tool call (shell command, file read or write, network host, fetch, web search, MCP or unknown tool) before it runs, with filesystem facts (program pins, symlinks, glob operands), session taint and repository policies; each decision carries its verdict, rule ids and reasons, and in JavaScript a frozen copy of the action decided. The SDK decides and the caller enforces: a denied call must not run, an `ask` runs only after a person approves that exact call; it is not a sandbox. Both packages decide every conformance fixture exactly as `openmoat guard` does, and carry the release's version. Examples with tests: `crates/openmoat-sdk/examples/agent_loop.rs`, `sdk/js/examples/agent-loop.js` ([#686](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/686)).

### Changed
- An `awk` program whose `|` is regex alternation or whose `>` sits inside a regex or a string (`awk '/^## |^# /{print}' f`, `awk '/>>>>>>> /{p=0}' f`, `awk '{print $1 " -> " $2}' f`) is read-only and allowed by `text-tools`; a pipe, a redirection, `system`, `getline`, `ENVIRON`, `close(`, `fflush(`, a program file and a program the shell expands a variable into (`awk "$prog"`) still ask ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).
- `perl -pi -e 's/a/b/g' FILE…` (also `-0pi`, `-pi.bak`, `-i -pe`, `-ni`, `-0777 -pi`, any delimiter or bracket pair, several substitutions) is an edit of the named files, decided by the path rules as `sed -i` is: a project file allows, `~/.zshrc` is `shell-rc`, a hook under `.git` asks. The `e` flag, interpolated code or variables, anything beside the substitutions, a `-M` module and a program in a shell variable are decided as any other inline program, and `perl -pe` without `-i` is unchanged ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).
- `git -c KEY=VALUE` with a key that never names a program (`user.name`, `user.email`, `commit.gpgsign=false`, `init.defaultBranch`, `advice.*`, `color.*`, `i18n.*`, `core.autocrlf`, `core.safecrlf`, `core.quotepath`, `pull.rebase`, `pull.ff`, `push.default`, `push.autoSetupRemote`, `fetch.prune`, `rebase.autostash`, `rebase.autosquash`, `merge.conflictstyle`, `diff.renames`, `log.date`, `status.short`, `gc.auto`) is no longer `git-internals`; every other key (`core.hooksPath`, `alias.*`, `core.editor`, `credential.helper`, `include.path`, `user.signingkey`, `commit.gpgsign=true`, …) still asks, and `git init --template=DIR` with a directory now asks too while an empty `--template=` does not ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).
- `env-poison` no longer sees an assignment whose value changes nothing: `GIT_EDITOR`, `GIT_SEQUENCE_EDITOR`, `EDITOR` or `VISUAL` set to `true`, `:` or `cat`, and `PATH` kept as `$PATH` or set to system directories only (`/usr/bin:/bin`, `/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`, `/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin`), prefixed, exported or given to `env -i`. `EDITOR` and `VISUAL` set to anything else (`EDITOR=/tmp/x`) are now denied like `GIT_EDITOR`, and a `PATH` that adds any other directory stays denied ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).
- `git branch -D NAME` and `git branch --delete --force NAME` ask (new group `destructive-recoverable`: the reflog holds the commits for weeks) instead of being denied by `destructive`, which keeps `git push --force`, `git push --delete`, `git reset --hard`, `rm -rf ~` and the rest; run `openmoat policy upgrade` to add the group to an existing policy ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).
- A `session-taint` ask is answered once per file: after you approve the agent's `Write`, `Edit` or `MultiEdit` of a protected file in a session that read untrusted content, later writes and edits of the same file in that session are allowed until the session reads untrusted content again (the reason names the earlier write). The evidence is the file itself, which must hold what the approved call leaves (for an edit, the file with its replacements applied) and not what it held before, so a declined write, a write of the file's own content, an edit Claude Code would refuse, a patch or shell write, another file or another session still ask (ADR-048; the audit database gains a column and migrates on first use) ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).
- A `curl` or `wget` download that sends nothing (`curl -o FILE URL`, `curl -O URL`, `wget -O FILE URL`, `wget URL`) is a `fetch` of the URL plus a write of the file: an unlisted host defers to the agent's own prompt like `WebFetch`, and the file is decided by the path rules (`curl -o ~/.zshrc …` is denied by `shell-rc`, `curl -o ~/.local/bin/x …` asks, a project file is allowed). `-q`, `--no-config --no-netrc` and `--max-redirect=0` are no longer required and redirects are followed; a download that sends data, uses `.netrc` or `--location-trusted`, or writes a file the line does not name stays `net`. Setting `CURL_HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `WGETRC` or `SYSTEM_WGETRC` is denied by `env-poison`, since it moves the configuration files that could turn such a download into an upload ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).
- New allow group `openmoat-readonly`: an agent may run `openmoat --help`, `-h`, `help`, `--version`, `-V`, `<command> --help`, `policy lint FILE`, `policy check …` and `demo` (also as `moat` and by path, such as a `target/debug/openmoat` build), which read no state and change nothing; `kernel-self` still denies every other person-only command, including the same commands under a pseudo-terminal wrapper. Run `openmoat policy upgrade` to add the group to an existing policy ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).

### Fixed
- `df -h ~` and `df /Users/me` no longer ask as a read of the home folder (`home-sweep`): `df` queries the file system holding a path and opens nothing under it; `du` still counts as a read ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).
- A `~` inside an interpreter's inline program (`perl -0pi -e 's~a~b~s'`) is read as the delimiter or operator it is, not as the home folder or another user's home, and the program word is no longer taken for a file operand ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).
- `pipe-to-shell` now matches only a consumer that runs its stdin as its program (`| sh`, `| python3`, `| python3 -`, `| node -`, `| perl`, `| source /dev/stdin`, `| xargs sh`), reported as the canonical `<stages> | <interpreter>` pipeline; `curl … | python3 -I -c CODE`, `| node -e CODE`, `| perl -ne CODE`, `| jq` and `| grep` read the download as data and are decided as before without the deny ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).
- `env | cut -d= -f1`, `env | cut -d = -f 1`, `printenv | sed 's/=.*//'`, `env | sed -E 's/=.*$//'` and `env | awk -F= '{print $1}'` are no longer denied as an environment dump (`env-dump`): the filter keeps the names and drops every value; any other consumer (`| grep`, `| sort`, `| head`, `| cut -d= -f2`, `| base64`, `| tee`) leaves the deny ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).
- A shell pattern's `*` no longer crosses `;`, `&&`, `||` or `&`, so `git push -u origin b 2>&1 | tail; git for-each-ref --format=…` is no longer denied as a force push (`destructive`) because of the later command's `--format=`; a force push anywhere on the line is still denied by its own command ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).
- `stops-openmoat` reads a `pkill`/`killall` pattern as the regular expression it is and asks only when it could match the process name `openmoat` or `moat`: `pkill -f latency-compare.py; bash moat-notes/run.sh` and `pkill -f my-server.js` no longer ask, while `pkill -f 'open.*'`, `pkill -f .`, `pkill -f 'o.*t'`, `pkill -x openmoat`, `killall moat` and `pkill -f /usr/local/bin/openmoat` do ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).
- A test of whether a secret variable is set is no longer a read of it (`env-secrets`): `[ -n "$GITHUB_TOKEN" ]`, `[ -z … ]`, `test -n`, `[[ -n $X ]]`, `${#X}`, `${X:+set}` and `${X+set}`; `echo $X`, `${X:0:4}`, `y=$X`, `[ "$X" = … ]` and `${X:+$X}` stay denied ([#693](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/693)).

## [0.11.0] - 2026-10-28

**Upgrading:** run `openmoat policy upgrade` in your own terminal to take the new default rules (`agent-credentials`, `protected-contents`, `session-written-code`, `opaque-code` and the new `kernel-self` and `shell-rc` paths), then `openmoat init` and restart your agents: OpenClaw, pi and OpenCode report their plugin loading only from a start after the upgrade, and `openmoat status` says "plugin not loaded yet" until then. The audit log moves to schema 5 on first use; an older OpenMoat refuses it, so keep one OpenMoat installed. OpenCode before 1.3.4 is no longer set up. The library crates change their API (marked **breaking** below); the `openmoat` command, the policy format and the hook payloads do not change.

### Security
- New default rule `agent-credentials` (hook only): the agent's own tools may neither read nor write the files where Claude Code, Codex, GitHub Copilot CLI, pi, Gemini CLI, OpenCode, Hermes Agent, OpenClaw and the Continue CLI keep their login, provider keys and OAuth tokens (OpenCode's `~/.local/share/opencode/auth.json`, Hermes Agent's `auth.json` and `mcp-tokens/`, Codex's `auth.json`, …), in a moved agent directory too; `pi auth print-api-key`, `pi auth print-bearer-token`, `opencode auth export` and `secret-tool lookup` are denied, and `claude setup-token` asks (`agent-token`). Under `openmoat run` each agent still reads its own login ([#666](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/666)).
- An agent running its own CLI to save an MCP server, turn one on or start an MCP OAuth sign-in (`claude mcp add`, `codex mcp add`, `gemini mcp add`, `opencode mcp add`/`auth`, `hermes mcp add`, …) asks (`runs-later`), as does a write of the Continue CLI's `~/.continue/config.yaml`; `mcp list` and `mcp get` are unchanged ([#666](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/666)).
- `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` warn, in one line, when an agent on the search path is a version with a published security advisory that a later version fixes, such as the OpenCode MCP OAuth command execution on Windows and WSL (1.0.137 to 1.18.32) and pi's `auth.json` write race (before 0.78.1) ([#666](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/666)).
- OpenCode's `OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR` and the Hermes Agent profiles `openmoat init` finds get the protection of the agent's own configuration directory; Hermes Agent's `memory` and `skill_manage` tools are checked as writes of its `memories/` and `skills/`, so the session-taint and `runs-later` asks apply; `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` also warn about a project's `opencode.jsonc`, `.opencode/opencode.jsonc` and `.opencode/plugin/` ([#666](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/666)).
- `moat guard` builds a path pattern's matcher only for a path that can begin as the pattern's literal start does, so a call that names a file no longer builds every file pattern of the policy; the verdicts are unchanged ([#666](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/666)).
- Reading or copying a folder that holds files a deny rule names now asks (`protected-contents`, ADR-038). Before, `cp -r ~/.codex /tmp/c`, `grep -r token ~/.gemini`, `tar czf a.tgz ~/.hermes`, `rsync -a ~/.local/share/opencode x`, `zip -r a.zip ~/.copilot` or `cp -r ~/.docker x` deferred: the classifier sees one read of the folder, and `agent-credentials` and `secrets-paths` name files inside it. Every deny rule's paths now protect the folders they lie in, in every spelling (moved agent directories, other case on macOS and Windows), for reads and for writes into the folder. `ls ~/.codex` and `ls ~/Library` ask too (the home folder and the folders above it are left to `home-sweep` and `destructive`, so `ls /` is unchanged); folders inside a project are unchanged, so `grep -r TODO .` stays allowed. Hosts that cannot ask deny it with the `openmoat allow --last` hint ([#670](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/670)).
- An extraction is a write of the folder it extracts into: `tar -x … -C DIR` (`--directory`, also `gtar` and `bsdtar`) and `unzip … -d DIR`, or the working directory without one. `tar xf e.tar -C ~/.claude` and `unzip e.zip -d ~/.moat` were deferred and are now denied by `kernel-self`; `tar xf e.tar -C ~` is denied by `destructive` ([#670](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/670)).
- `git checkout [<tree-ish>] [--] <path>…` writes each path, as `git restore` does: `git checkout main -- .github/workflows/ci.yml` and `git checkout .claude/hooks/x.sh` ask (`runs-later`) instead of deferring or being allowed, and `git checkout -- ~/.claude/settings.json` is denied; `git checkout -- src/main.rs` and `git checkout main` are unchanged ([#670](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/670)).
- `shell-rc` also denies writes to fish's startup files (`~/.config/fish/config.fish`, `conf.d/`, `functions/`), the login and logout files (`~/.zlogin`, `~/.zlogout`, `~/.bash_login`, `~/.bash_logout`, `~/.login`), csh, tcsh, ksh and mksh startup files (`~/.cshrc`, `~/.tcshrc`, `~/.kshrc`, `~/.mkshrc`) and the drop-in folders `~/.bashrc.d/` and `~/.zshrc.d/`, which run as often as `~/.zshrc` and were deferred; the host sandboxes deny them too. Reading them is unchanged ([#671](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/671)).
- Writing into a folder on `PATH` (`~/.local/bin`, `~/bin`, `~/.cargo/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`, `/opt/homebrew/bin`) now asks (`persistence`): a file there named after `git`, `ls` or `node` runs in its place later. `install -m755 evil ~/.local/bin/git` and `ln -sf evil ~/.local/bin/git` were deferred. Installers (`pipx install`, `uv tool install`, `cargo install`, `brew install`, `npm i -g`) are commands, not writes, and are unchanged ([#671](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/671)).
- `env-dump` also denies inline Python that reaches the environment through `__import__('os')` (`python3 -c 'print(__import__("os").environ)'`, `__import__("os").__dict__`), which was deferred. An alias (`e = os.environ; print(e)`) is still not followed ([#671](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/671)).
- `kill` of the pids a command substitution naming `moat` looks up now asks like `pkill openmoat` (`stops-openmoat`): `kill -9 $(pgrep openmoat)`, `kill $(pidof moat)` and `` kill `pgrep -f openmoat` `` were deferred. `pgrep openmoat` alone and `kill $(pgrep node)` are unchanged ([#671](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/671)).
- Writing what every Python start runs now asks (`runs-later`), the allowed `pytest` included: a `.pth` file in a `site-packages` or `dist-packages` directory, and `sitecustomize.py` or `usercustomize.py` anywhere, a project's `.venv` and `src/` included. They were deferred outside the project and allowed inside it. Session taint protects them too; the host sandboxes leave them writable, since installs write them ([#671](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/671)).
- Running a file the same agent session wrote now asks where no OS sandbox from OpenMoat holds the command (`session-written-code`, ADR-039): on OpenClaw, GitHub Copilot CLI, pi, Gemini CLI, OpenCode and Hermes Agent, and on Claude Code and Cursor on native Windows or with their sandbox not in place. Before, an agent could write `x.py` and run `python3 x.py`, `node x.js` or `go run x.go` (allowed) or `bash x.sh`, `ruby x.rb` or `perl x.pl` (deferred), and the script could read `~/.ssh/id_rsa` through a path it builds. Every interpreter form (`python3 -u x.py`, `python -m tool`, `node -r ./x.js`, `deno run`, `bun`, `php -f`, `pwsh -File`, `npx tsx`, `sh < x.sh`, `source x`), a program named by a path (`./x`, `bin/x`), and inline code importing a module the session wrote (`python3 -c 'import tool'`) count, through `env`, `time`, `bash -c` and the other wrappers; so do shell writes (`echo … > x.sh; bash x.sh`). Claude Code, Codex and Cursor with their sandbox in place on macOS and Linux, and `openmoat run`, are unchanged. A session grant (`openmoat allow --last`) answers it until the session rewrites the file; OpenCode 1 denies it with that hint. Files the session did not write (`python manage.py`) and test runners (`pytest`, `npm test`, `cargo test`) keep their verdict. The audit log moves to schema 5, which records each session's writes, bounded to 30 days and 100 000 files ([#672](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/672)).
- Inline interpreter code that opens files, connects, reads the environment or runs programs with text it builds at run time now asks (`opaque-code`), with `-c`/`-e` and in a here-document alike: `python3 -c 'print(open(os.path.expanduser("~/.s"+"sh/id_rsa")).read())'`, `node -e "require('fs').readFileSync(process.env.HOME+'/.ss'+'h/id_rsa')"` and `perl -e 'open F,"$ENV{HOME}/.s"."sh/id_rsa";print <F>'` deferred before. `python3 -c 'print(1+1)'`, `node -e 'console.log(2)'` and code that hands those APIs literal text keep their verdict ([#672](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/672)).
- Inline interpreter code that writes a file now writes every file name it shows, not only reads it: `python3 -c 'open("/Users/me/.zshrc","a").write("x")'` and its Node, Ruby, Perl, PHP, Deno and Lua equivalents are denied by `shell-rc` (they were deferred as reads), writes of `~/.claude/settings.json` by `kernel-self`, a workflow written that way asks (`runs-later`), and a script written by a one-liner counts as written by the session for `session-written-code`. Code that only reads keeps its verdict ([#672](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/672)).
- OpenClaw now runs the parameters OpenMoat decided on (ADR-041). OpenClaw shows each `before_tool_call` handler the call as it came and runs the `params` of the last one that returns some, so another plugin could turn a checked `git status` into `cat ~/.ssh/id_rsa` and that ran. OpenMoat's plugin now decides at the highest priority and returns the parameters it checked at the lowest, which undoes such a change; a plugin that changes a call and asks for approval in the same answer keeps its change once a person approves it, as THREAT_MODEL §5 says ([#674](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/674)).
- A plugin its agent did not load no longer looks protected (ADR-042). OpenClaw, pi and OpenCode start without a plugin that fails to load and then run every call unchecked. OpenMoat's plugins now run `openmoat guard --heartbeat --host <id>` as they load, and `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` say "plugin not loaded yet" (a note) until the plugin has reported or sent a call since `openmoat init` wrote it or the agent was updated; when pi wrote a session since without either, they warn and report pi as not protected. `openmoat status --format json` lists it under `plugins`. The default policy denies an agent running `openmoat guard --heartbeat` itself (`kernel-self`), so one agent cannot report another's plugin as loaded ([#674](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/674)).
- An entry of OpenMoat's without the agent's fail-closed key (`fail_closed: true` for Hermes Agent, `failClosed: true` for Cursor) is reported as not protected, with the reason, instead of "out of date": the agent would run a call whose hook failed. `openmoat init` puts the key back ([#674](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/674)).
- Hermes Agent's limits are documented from its source: its `plugins.hook_callback_timeout` (30 s by default) blocks a call whose hook has not answered, before the entry's own 60 s and together with the guard's own 30 s, so `openmoat init` leaves it alone; and Hermes runs a call unchecked when its own hook dispatcher raises an error ([#674](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/674)).
- The OpenClaw, pi and OpenCode plugins are type-checked (`// @ts-check`, strict, `scripts/ci/check-js.sh`) and each runs under Node against a stand-in for its host and for the guard in the tests: every failure of the guard blocks, a deny, ask, allow and no answer reach the agent as they must, and the OpenClaw rewrite is undone ([#674](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/674)).

### Added
- Hermes Agent is a supported agent (`openmoat init --hosts hermes`, hook only): one `pre_tool_call` hook with `fail_closed: true` in its `config.yaml`, edited as text so your comments stay, and its command approved in `shell-hooks-allowlist.json`, without which Hermes's gateway, cron and ACP sessions skip it; `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` report a hook the allowlist does not approve. An ask is Hermes's own approval prompt, and a deny you approve with `openmoat allow --last` where Hermes would approve it unseen (YOLO mode, `approvals.mode: off`). The lock pins both files, the MCP servers in `mcp_servers` one by one, and `kernel-self` protects Hermes's configuration, allowlist, `.env`, plugins and hooks and denies `HERMES_SAFE_MODE` ([#665](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/665)).
- OpenCode 1 (`opencode-ai`) is a supported agent, host `opencode`, hook only: `openmoat init` writes OpenMoat's plugin to `~/.config/opencode/plugins/openmoat.js` (or `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/opencode`), which checks every tool call through `tool.execute.before` and blocks the call when `openmoat` is missing, fails or takes over 45 s. OpenCode 1 cannot ask, so an `ask` is a deny that says how to approve the call. The default policy denies the agent changing the plugin and the files that load plugins, starting OpenCode with `--pure` or `OPENCODE_PURE`, `opencode upgrade --major` and `opencode plugin --global`, and asks before it writes other plugins, custom tools, commands or a project's `opencode.json`. OpenCode 2 support follows in the next entry ([#665](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/665)).
- OpenCode 2 (`@opencode/cli`) is supported, through the same `opencode` host and one plugin for both versions (ADR-040): OpenMoat's plugin exports `{ id, server, setup }`, so OpenCode 1 (1.3.4 and later) runs it on `tool.execute.before` and OpenCode 2 on `execute.before`, and an upgrade from 1 to 2 needs no new `openmoat init`; `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` name the hook the `opencode` on the search path runs. On OpenCode 2 an `ask` is OpenCode's own permission prompt (OpenCode 1 still receives it as a deny with the `openmoat allow --last` hint), and the plugin replaces the call's `tool` and `input` with what it checked, frozen, so another plugin that rewrites the call afterwards makes it fail; the shell command it checked is pinned the same way when OpenCode starts it. OpenCode 2's tools (`shell`, `read`/`write`/`edit` with `path`, `patch`, `opencode_worktree_remove`, …), its `~` expansion and its `mcp.servers` are read. `-openmoat` (or `-*`) in OpenCode 2's `plugins`, or a policy denying the plugin, makes `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` report OpenCode as not protected (the person's own `opencode.json[c]`) or warn about the project; the default policy denies the agent setting `OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR`, `OPENCODE_CONFIG` or `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`, writing OpenCode 2's `service*.json`, and running `opencode plugin add|remove|update` or `opencode service set|unset`. With `OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR` set, `openmoat init` installs the plugin there, which both versions load. OpenCode before 1.3.4, which loads no object plugin, is no longer set up: `openmoat status` says to upgrade ([#673](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/673)).

### Changed
- **Breaking** for the library crates: `openmoat-hosts` replaces `InstallSpec::unsupported` with `InstallSpec::versions` (the oldest version that loads OpenMoat's plugin and the hook each major runs) and adds `InstallSpec::heartbeat` and `HookFile::fail_closed_key`; `openmoat-audit` adds the session-writes table (schema 5) and `Store::host_seen_since`; `openmoat-core` adds `CompiledPolicy::executed` ([#672](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/672), [#673](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/673), [#674](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/674)).

### Fixed
- `.env.dist` and `.env.<mode>.example` templates are no longer blocked by `secrets-paths` ([#668](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/668)).
- `openmoat doctor --format json` names GitHub Copilot CLI's checks after the agent: a name as wide as the text output's column ran into its detail, so tools reading the JSON (OpenMoat Bar) did not find the agent's protection line ([#683](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/683)).

## [0.10.1] - 2026-10-24

**Upgrading:** nothing to run; the policy format, the commands and the lock are unchanged.

### Security
- The hook follows a chain of dangling symlinks to its end up to 64 links, past every operating system's own limit, so a write through a 9-link chain into `~/.ssh` is checked where it lands; a session grant for a file that no longer resolves (a link loop, a longer chain) no longer holds and the call is asked again ([#658](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/658)).
- The egress proxy looks a session's taint up again on every recheck while it cannot read the audit log's newest event, instead of reusing the taint it last saw for up to the recheck age; a lookup that then fails still refuses the connection ([#658](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/658)).

### Changed
- `openmoat init` sums up each file it would change for an agent (new or edited, lines added and removed, the top-level settings it sets) instead of printing the whole diff before asking; answer `d` at `Protect …? [Y/n/d]` to see the full diff ([#663](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/663)).
- `openmoat status` lists the agents it finds no folder for on one `not found` line instead of one line each; `openmoat doctor` keeps one line per agent ([#663](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/663)).

### Fixed
- Rules naming `~` match when the home directory is spelled with a doubled or trailing slash (`HOME=/Users//me/`): `openmoat demo`, which resolves no symlinks, showed a key read under such a home as `defer` instead of `deny` ([#663](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/663)).

## [0.10.0] - 2026-10-23

**Upgrading:** nothing to run for a default install; the policy lock keeps verifying. An OpenClaw configuration at another path (`OPENCLAW_CONFIG_PATH`) shows drift once: run `openmoat init`. `openmoat-hosts` changes its library API (marked **breaking** below); the `openmoat` command, the policy format and the hook payloads do not change.

### Security
- A session grant for a file holds only while the file resolves where it did when approved: a granted file replaced by a symlink to another file is asked about again ([#639](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/639)).
- An open proxy tunnel is checked against a session taint that includes every event recorded since it was last looked up, so it closes at the next recheck after the session reads a secret, not up to one interval later ([#639](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/639)).
- pi's `read` names each spelling pi may open when the path does not exist (` AM.`/` PM.` with a narrow no-break space, `'` as `’`), so a rule on any of them decides ([#639](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/639)).
- Claude Code's `Artifact` read of a page is a fetch of its URL, so it taints the session with untrusted content like `WebFetch`; a relative `file_path` with `root` is read both from the working directory and below `root` ([#639](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/639)).
- The policy lock pins a file without its `mcp.servers` (whose servers the MCP pins hold one by one) only when the file is OpenClaw's own `openclaw.json`, recorded per file in the lock (`pinned_forms`), no longer for any pinned file whose contents look like an OpenClaw configuration: another host's file that held OpenMoat's plugin entry and `mcp.servers` changed its servers without drift. A lock written before this has no `pinned_forms` and keeps verifying: a pinned file named `openclaw.json` is read as OpenClaw's, every other file by its bytes, so an OpenClaw configuration at another name (`OPENCLAW_CONFIG_PATH`) shows drift once until `openmoat init` or `openmoat doctor --accept` re-pins it; the digests themselves are unchanged ([#653](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/653)).

### Changed
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): `McpSpec` takes readers (`McpReader`) for its user and project files and `tool_names: &dyn McpNames`; `McpLayout` and `McpToolNames` are removed, and each host's MCP reader and tool naming live in its folder ([#640](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/640)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): a host spec says how to find its configuration (`InstallSpec::location`, `Location::Custom(&dyn Locate)`) and the form of its file the lock pins (`InstallSpec::pinned_form`); OpenClaw's location rules move into its folder, and `RelaxedKey`/`KeyShape` move to `contract/relaxed.rs` ([#641](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/641)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): `ProjectSettingsSpec::reader` (a `SettingsReader` over a parsed `SettingsFile`) replaces `format`/`SettingsFormat`, and each host's project-settings reader lives in its folder ([#642](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/642)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): host specs carry the facts the CLI used to branch on per host: `Asks::{ungoverned, allow_last_hint, prompt_free_modes}`, `HostSpec::{resume_hint, detect}`, `InstallSpec::extra_files` and `SandboxSpec::tiocsti_open_on_linux`; `Asks` moves to `contract/asks.rs` with `Asks::BASE` ([#643](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/643)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): a `HookFile` trait (pure edits on the parsed hook file: entry, ours, binary, upsert, state, remove) holds the nested, Cursor, Gemini CLI and GitHub Copilot CLI hook-file layouts in the hosts crate, reached through `HookFormat::hook_file`; the CLI's `install/hook_file.rs` only reads, backs up and writes ([#644](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/644)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API): host specs carry the paths of their own files the default policy's `kernel-self` rule and the taint list protect (`HostSpec::protect`, `Protect`, `Paths`) and how to tell their processes (`HostSpec::process`, `ProcessSpec`, `Helper`); OpenClaw's, GitHub Copilot CLI's, pi's and Gemini CLI's lines in those lists are generated from the specs, byte for byte as before, and checked by a test ([#646](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/646)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): `InstallSpec::hook_file` (a `HookLayout`: `Entries` with a `HookFile`, `Owned` or `Plugin` with an `OwnedFile`) and `InstallSpec::dir_variable_is_home` replace `hook_format`/`HookFormat`; pi's extension and OpenClaw's plugin move into their host folders (`host/<id>/integration/`), unchanged ([#646](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/646)).
- Each host's golden payloads move from `tests/fixtures/hosts/<id>/` to its folder, `crates/openmoat-hosts/src/host/<module>/fixtures/`, unchanged; `Host::module` names that folder ([#646](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/646)).
- Contributor docs: a `HOST.md` per agent in its host folder, an `AGENTS.md` per crate, changelog fragments in `changelog.d/` folded into this file at release time (`scripts/changelog-assemble.py`, checked by `scripts/changelog-check.py`), and the 500-line cap counting code lines only ([#646](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/646)).
- The MIT license text is now `LICENSE` (it was `LICENSE-MIT`), beside `LICENSE-APACHE`; the project is still dual-licensed `MIT OR Apache-2.0`, and the file name is the one license scanners look for ([#651](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/651)).

### Fixed
- `openmoat doctor` no longer says OpenClaw's plugin file "is pinned but not this shell's": it counts the files the lock pins with a host's hook file, as `openmoat status` does ([#646](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/646)).
